## I / 一



===Original===
Dick Martin’s last official job (as he believed) was to pull in Lew Pheeney, who was wanted in connection with the Helborough bank robbery. He found Lew in a little Soho café, just as he was finishing his coffee.

===Chinese===
迪克·马丁（Dick Martin）最后一桩公务（他自己也这么认为），就是逮捕卢·菲尼（Lew Pheeney），此人因涉嫌赫尔堡罗（Helborough）银行劫案而被通缉。他在苏荷区（Soho）一家小咖啡馆里找到了卢，那人正喝完咖啡。

===Original===
“What’s the idea, colonel?” asked Lew, almost genially, as he got his hat.
“The inspector wants to talk to you about that Helborough job,” said Dick.

===Chinese===
“这是什么路数，‘上校’？”卢取帽子的时候问道，语气近乎亲热。
“督察想找你谈谈赫尔堡罗那桩案子。”迪克说。

===Original===
Lew’s nose wrinkled in contempt.
“Helborough grandmothers!” he said scornfully. “I’m out of that bank business﻿—thought you knew it. What are you doing in the force, Martin? They told me that you’d run into money and had quit.”

===Chinese===
卢的鼻子轻蔑地皱了起来。
“去他的赫尔堡罗！”他不屑地说，“银行那档子事我早就不沾了——还当你知道呢。你怎么还在警队当差，马丁？他们说你发了一笔财，已经不干了。”

===Original===
“I’m quitting. You’re my last bit of business.”
“Too bad you’re falling down on the last lap!” grinned Lew. “I’ve got forty-five well-oiled alibis. I’m surprised at you, Martin. You know I don’t ‘blow’ banks; locks are my speciality﻿—”

===Chinese===
“我这就不干了。你是我经手的最后一桩事。”
“可惜你在最后一棒上栽了跟头！”卢咧嘴笑道，“我有四十五条滑溜顺当的不在场证明。你真让我吃惊，马丁。你知道我从不‘炸’银行；开锁才是我的本行——”

===Original===
“What were you doing at ten o’clock on Tuesday night?”
A broad smile illuminated the homely face of the burglar.

===Chinese===
“星期二晚上十点钟，你在干什么？”
窃贼那张其貌不扬的脸上绽开了一个灿烂的笑容。

===Original===
“If I told you, you’d think I was lying.”
“Give me a chance,” pleaded Dick, his blue eyes twinkling.

===Chinese===
“我要是说了，你会当我是在撒谎。”
“给我个机会嘛。”迪克央告道，蓝眼睛里闪着光。

===Original===
Lew did not reply at once. He seemed to be pondering the dangers of too great frankness. But when he had seen all sides of the matter, he spoke the truth.

===Chinese===
卢没有立刻回答。他似乎在掂量过分坦白的风险。等他把这件事的方方面面都盘算遍了，才说出了实话。

===Original===
“I was doing a private job﻿—a job I don’t want to talk about. It was dirty, but honest.”

===Chinese===
“我在干一桩私活——一桩我不愿多谈的活儿。那活儿肮脏，倒不犯法。”

===Original===
“And were you well paid?” asked his captor, polite but incredulous.

===Chinese===
“报酬优厚吗？”抓他的这一位问道，客气里透着不信。

===Original===
“I was﻿—I got one hundred and fifty pounds on account. That makes you jump, but it is the truth. I was picking locks, certainly the toughest locks I’ve ever struck, and it was a kind of horrible job I wouldn’t do again for a carload of money. You don’t believe me, but I can prove that I spent the night at the Royal Arms, Chichester, that I was there at eight o’clock to dinner and at eleven o’clock to sleep. So you can forget all that Helborough bank stuff. I know the gang that did it, and you know ’em too, and we don’t change cards.”

===Chinese===
“优厚——我先到手了一百五十镑。这话吓你一跳，可千真万确。我在开锁，那绝对是我这辈子碰上过的最难啃的锁，那活儿邪门得很，给我一汽车的钱我也不再干第二回。你不信，可我能证明那晚我是在奇切斯特（Chichester）的皇家纹章旅馆（Royal Arms）过的夜，八点钟在那儿吃的晚饭，十一点钟上的床。所以，赫尔堡罗银行那摊子事你就省省吧。我知道是哪一伙人干的，你也知道，咱们谁也不亮牌。”

===Original===
They kept Lew in the cells all night whilst inquiries were pursued. Remarkably enough, he had not only stayed at the Royal Arms at Chichester, but had stayed in his own name; and it was true that at a quarter to eleven, before the Helborough bank robbers had left the premises, he was taking a drink in his room, sixty miles away. So authority released Lew in the morning and Dick went into breakfast with him, because, between the professional thief-taker and the professional burglar there is no real ill-feeling, and Sub-Inspector Richard Martin was almost as popular with the criminal classes as he was at police headquarters.

===Chinese===
卢在拘留室里被关了一夜，期间警方四下查证。说来也怪，他不仅确曾在奇切斯特的皇家纹章旅馆投宿，而且登记的还是本名；同样确凿的是，十点三刻，赫尔堡罗银行的劫匪们还没离开现场，他已经在六十英里外自己的房间里喝上了酒。于是当局一早就把卢放了，迪克还同他一道去吃了早餐，因为在职业捕快和职业窃贼之间并没有真正的恶感，而副督察理查德·马丁在匪类当中的人缘，几乎不亚于他在警察总部的声望。

===Original===
“No, Mr. Martin, I’m not going to tell you anything more than I’ve already told you,” said Lew good-humouredly. “And when you call me a liar, I’m not so much as hurt in my feelings. I got a hundred an’ fifty pounds, and I’d have got a thousand if I’d pulled it off. You can guess all round it, but you’ll never guess right.”

===Chinese===
“不，马丁先生，除了我已经告诉你的，我什么也不会再多说。”卢和颜悦色地说，“你当我是撒谎，我心里也谈不上多委屈。我拿到了一百五十镑，要是那事儿办成了，还能拿一千。你尽管围着这事猜，可你永远猜不中。”

===Original===
Dick Martin was eyeing him keenly.
“You’ve got a good story in your mind﻿—spill it,” he said.

===Chinese===
迪克·马丁正锐利地打量着他。
“你肚子里揣着个好听的故事——倒出来吧。”他说。

===Original===
He waited suggestively, but Lew Pheeney shook his head.
“I’m not telling. The story would give away a man who’s not a good fellow, and not one I admire; but I can’t let my personal feelings get the better of me, and you’ll have to go on guessing. And I’m not lying. I’ll tell you how it happened.”

===Chinese===
他满怀期待地等着，可卢·菲尼直摇头。
“我不说。这故事会把一个家伙给抖搂出去，那家伙不是什么好东西，我也不佩服他；可我不能让私人感情占了上风，你只好接着猜。我没有撒谎。我可以把事情的经过讲给你听。”

===Original===
He gulped down a cup of hot coffee and pushed cup and saucer away from him.

===Chinese===
他一口气灌下一杯热咖啡，把杯碟推到一旁。

===Original===
“I don’t know this fellow who asked me to do the work﻿—not personally. He’s been in trouble for something or other, but that’s no business of mine. One night he met me, introduced himself, and I went to his house﻿—br-r!” he shivered. “Martin, a crook is a pretty clean man﻿—at least, all the crooks I know; and thieving’s just a game with two players; me and the police. If they snooker me, good luck to ’em! If I can beat them, good luck to me! But there’s some dirt that makes me sick, just makes my stomach turn over. When he told me the job he wanted me for, I thought he was joking, and my first idea was to turn it in right away. But I’m just the most curious creature that ever lived, and it was a new experience, so, after a lot of think, I said ‘Yes.’ Mind you, there was nothing dishonest in it. All he wanted to do was to take a peep at something. What was behind it I don’t know. I don’t want to talk about it, but the locks beat me.”

===Chinese===
“托我干这活儿的家伙我不认识——素不相识。他为这样那样的事吃过官司，可那与我无关。一天夜里他找上我，自报了家门，我就去了他家——嚯！”他打了个寒战。“马丁，贼是挺干净的人——至少我认得的贼都这样；偷东西不过是一场两个人的游戏：我和警察。他们设局算计了我，算他们走运！我要是赢了他们，算我走运！可有些肮脏事真让我恶心，直叫我反胃。他跟我说要我干的那活儿时，我还当他开玩笑，头一个念头就是当场回绝。可我偏偏是天底下头一号好奇的人，那又是桩新鲜经历，于是左思右想之后，我说了声‘好’。提醒你一句，那活儿没有任何不清不白的地方。他不过是想看一眼什么东西。背后藏着什么名堂，我不知道。我不愿多谈，反正那几把锁把我难住了。”

===Original===
“A lawyer’s safe?” suggested the interested detective.

===Chinese===
“律师的保险柜？”听得来了兴致的侦探提示道。

===Original===
The other shook his head. He turned the subject abruptly; spoke of his plans﻿—he was leaving for the United States, to join his brother, who was an honest builder.

===Chinese===
对方摇了摇头。他陡然岔开话题，谈起自己的打算——他要动身去美国投奔兄弟，他兄弟是个本本分分的营造商。

===Original===
“We’re both going out of the game together, Martin,” he smiled. “You’re too good a man for a policeman, and I’m too much of a gentleman to be on the crook. I shouldn’t be surprised if we met one of these days.”

===Chinese===
“咱们俩这是要一块儿退出这一行喽，马丁。”他笑道，“你当警察，人太好了；我做贼，绅士气又太重。哪天咱们再碰上面，我也不会奇怪。”

===Original===
Dick went back to the Yard to make, as he thought, a final report to his immediate chief.

===Chinese===
迪克回到苏格兰场（Scotland Yard），打算向直属上司作他自认为的最后一次汇报。

===Original===
Captain Sneed sniffed.

===Chinese===
斯尼德上尉（Captain Sneed）嗤了嗤鼻子。

===Original===
“That Lew Pheeney couldn’t fall straight,” he said; “if you dropped him down a well, he’d wear away the brickwork. Honest robber! He’s got that out of a book. You think you’ve finished work, I suppose?”

===Chinese===
“那个卢·菲尼，天生就歪，想直着掉下去都难。”他说，“你把他扔进井里，他能把井壁的砖都磨穿。老实的劫匪！这词儿他准是从哪本书里学来的。我猜，你以为自己已经交差了事？”

===Original===
Dick nodded.
“Going to buy a country house and be a gentleman. Ride to hounds and take duchesses into dinner﻿—what a hell of a life for a grown man!”

===Chinese===
迪克点了点头。
“打算买座乡间宅邸，当个绅士。骑马猎狐，挽着公爵夫人入席——一个大男人竟要过这种日子，真是活见鬼！”

===Original===
Dick Martin grinned at the sneer. He wanted very little persuasion to withdraw his resignation; already he was repenting﻿—and, despite the attraction of authorship which beckoned ahead, he would have given a lot of money to recall the letter he had sent to the commissioner.

===Chinese===
迪克·马丁对这番讥诮咧嘴一笑。要劝他收回辞呈，几乎用不着费什么口舌；他本来就在后悔——虽说前方招手的是当作家的诱人前景，他还是情愿花上一大笔钱，追回寄给总监的那封辞呈。

===Original===
“It’s a queer thing how money ruins a man,” said Captain Sneed sadly. “Now if I had a six-figure legacy I should want to do nothing.”

===Chinese===
“钱这东西毁人，真是桩怪事。”斯尼德上尉悲伤地说，“我要是得了一笔六位数的遗产，就什么都不想干了。”

===Original===
His assistant might sneer in turn.
“You want to do nothing, anyway,” he said; “you’re lazy, Sneed﻿—the laziest man who ever filled a chair at Scotland Yard.”

===Chinese===
他的助手便反唇相讥。
“你本来就什么都不想干。”他说，“你懒，斯尼德——在苏格兰场坐过班的人里，数你顶懒。”

===Original===
The fat man, who literally filled and overflowed the padded office chair in which he half sat and half lay, a picture of inertia, raised his reproachful eyes to his companion.

===Chinese===
胖子半坐半躺在一把蒙着软垫的办公椅里，实实在在把椅子填满还往外溢，活脱脱一幅怠惰图。他抬起一双责备的眼睛望着同伴。

===Original===
“Insubordination,” he murmured. “You’re not out of the force till tomorrow﻿—call me ‘sir’ and be respectful. I hate reminding you that you’re a paltry sub-inspector and that I’m as near being a superintendent as makes no difference. It would sound snobbish. I’m not lazy, I’m lethargic. It’s a sort of disease.”

===Chinese===
“以下犯上。”他喃喃道，“到明天你才算脱离警队——先叫一声‘长官’，放规矩点。我真不愿意提醒你：你不过是个区区副督察，而我嘛，跟警司也只差一步，几乎没分别。这话听着像摆架子。我不懒，我是倦怠。这是一种病。”

===Original===
“You’re fat because you’re lazy, and you’re lazy because you’re fat,” insisted the lean-faced young man. “It’s a sort of vicious circle. Besides, you’re rich enough to retire if you wanted.”

===Chinese===
“你胖是因为你懒，你懒是因为你胖。”瘦脸的年轻人坚持道，“这是个恶性循环。再说，你富得都够退休了，只要你想退。”

===Original===
Captain Sneed stroked his chin reflectively. He was a giant of a man, with shoulders of an ox and the height of a Grenadier, but he was admittedly inert. He sighed heavily, and, groping in a desk basket, produced a blue paper.

===Chinese===
斯尼德上尉若有所思地摸着下巴。他是个巨人般的汉子，生着公牛般的肩膀、掷弹卫兵般的个头，无奈公认地不爱动弹。他沉沉地叹了口气，在公文筐里摸索了一阵，抽出一张蓝纸。

===Original===
“You’re a common civilian tomorrow﻿—but my slave today. Go along to Bellingham Library; there has been a complaint about stolen books.”

===Chinese===
“明天你就是平头百姓了——今天还得给我当苦力。跑一趟贝灵厄姆图书馆（Bellingham Library）吧；有人投诉丢了书。”

===Original===
Sub-Inspector Dick Martin groaned.

===Chinese===
副督察迪克·马丁哀叹一声。

===Original===
“It’s not romantic, I admit,” said his superior with a slow, broad smile; “kleptomania belongs to the dust and debris of detective work, but it is good for your soul. It will remind you, whilst you’re loafing on the money you didn’t earn, that there are a few thousand of your poor comrades wearin’ their feet into ankles with fool inquiries like this!”

===Chinese===
“我承认，这不浪漫。”他的上司慢慢咧开一个大大的笑容，“盗窃癖属于侦探行当里的尘土碎屑，可它对你的灵魂有好处。它会提醒你：当你靠着不是自己挣来的钱游手好闲时，还有几千个可怜的同僚，正为这类愚蠢的查访把脚板磨得只剩脚踝！”

===Original===
Dick (or “Slick” as he was called for certain reasons) wondered as he walked slowly down the long corridor whether he was glad or sorry that police work lay behind him and that on the morrow he might pass the most exalted official without saluting. He was a “larceny man,” the cleverest taker of thieves the Yard had known. Sneed often said that he had the mind of a thief, and meant this as a compliment. He certainly had the skill. There was a memorable night when, urged thereto by the highest police official in London, he had picked the pocket of a Secretary of State, taken his watch, his pocketbook and his private papers, and not even the expert watchers saw him perform the fell deed.

===Chinese===
迪克——出于某些缘故，人家都管他叫“滑头”（Slick）——沿着长长的走廊缓缓走着，心里琢磨着：警界生涯已成过去，明天起哪怕迎面遇上最高长官也可以不敬礼，这究竟是该高兴还是该惋惜。他是个“窃案侦探”，苏格兰场有史以来最高明的捉贼高手。斯尼德常说，他长着一颗贼的头脑，而这是句恭维话。他的确有这份本事。有个令人难忘的夜晚，伦敦警界最高的长官撺掇他露一手，他扒了一位国务大臣的口袋，取走了人家的怀表、皮夹和私人文件，连那些行家监视都没瞧见他如何干下这桩大胆的勾当。

===Original===
Dick Martin came to the Yard from Canada, where his father had been governor of a prison. He was neither a good guardian of criminals or youth. Dick had the run of the prison, and could take a stick pin from a man’s cravat before he had mastered the mysteries of algebra. Peter du Bois, a lifer, taught him to open almost any kind of door with a bent hairpin; Lew Andrevski, a frequent visitor to Fort Stuart, made a specially small pack of cards out of the covers of the chapel prayer books, in order that the lad should be taught to conceal three cards in each tiny palm. If he had not been innately honest, the tuition might easily have ruined him.

===Chinese===
迪克·马丁是从加拿大进的苏格兰场，他父亲在那边当过监狱典狱长。这位典狱长看罪犯看不住，看孩子也看不住。迪克在监狱里跑进跑出随意得很，还没弄明白代数的奥秘，就已经能从人家的领巾上摘下饰针了。彼得·杜布瓦（Peter du Bois），一个无期徒刑犯，教会他用一根弯发卡打开几乎任何一种门；卢·安德列夫斯基（Lew Andrevski），斯图尔特堡（Fort Stuart）的常客，用教堂祈祷书的封面做了一副特别小的纸牌，好教这孩子学会在每只小手心里各藏三张牌。若不是天性正直，这番调教十有八九会毁了他。

===Original===
“Dicky’s all right﻿—he can’t know too much of that crook stuff,” said the indolent Captain Martin, when his horrified relatives expostulated at the corruption of the motherless boy. “The boy’s like him﻿—he’s going into the police and the education’s worth a million!”

===Chinese===
“迪基（Dicky）没事的——那些歪门邪道，他懂得再多也不碍事。”懒散的马丁上尉（Captain Martin）说。那时，吓坏了的亲戚们纷纷抗议，说这个没了娘的孩子要被教坏了。“这孩子随他——将来是要进警察界的，这门教育值一百万！”

===Original===
Straight of body, clear-eyed, immensely sane, Dick Martin came happily through a unique period of test to the office. The war brought him to England, a stripling with a record of good work behind him. Scotland Yard claimed him, and he had the distinction of being the only member of the Criminal Investigation Department who had been appointed without going through a probationary period of patrol work.

===Chinese===
迪克·马丁身板挺直、目光清明、头脑无比健全，顺顺当当地通过了一段独一无二的考验期，走马上任。大战把他带到英国，虽是少年郎，身后已有一串出色的业绩。苏格兰场把他要了去，他还享有一项殊荣：刑事调查部（Criminal Investigation Department）里，唯有他是没经过巡逻见习期就直接获任的成员。

===Original===
As he went down the stone stairs, he was overtaken by the third commissioner.
“Hello, Martin! You’re leaving us tomorrow? Bad luck! It is a thousand pities you have money. We’re losing a good man. What are you going to do?”

===Chinese===
他走下石头楼梯时，第三总监（third commissioner）从后面赶了上来。
“你好啊，马丁！明天就要离开我们了？真不走运！你偏偏有钱，万分可惜。我们损失了一员好手。你打算去做什么？”

===Original===
Dick smiled ruefully.
“I don’t know﻿—I’m beginning to think I’ve made a mistake in leaving at all.”

===Chinese===
迪克苦笑了一下。
“不知道——我已经开始觉得，辞职这事儿本来就是走错了棋。”

===Original===
The “old man” nodded.
“Do anything except lecture,” he said, “and, for the Lord’s sake, don’t start a private agency! In America detective agencies do wonderful things﻿—in England their work is restricted to thinking up evidence for divorces. A man asked me only today if I could recommend﻿—”

===Chinese===
“老头子”点了点头。
“干什么都行，就是别去讲课。”他说，“千万看在老天的份上，别开私家侦探社！在美国，侦探公司能干出惊天动地的大事——在英国，它们的业务只限于给离婚案编造证据。就在今天，还有个人问我能不能推荐——”

===Original===
He stopped suddenly at the foot of the stairs and viewed Dick with a new interest.
“By Jove! I wonder﻿—! Do you know Havelock, the lawyer?”

===Chinese===
他在楼梯脚下突然停住，带着新的兴味打量起迪克来。
“哎呀！我琢磨——！你认识律师哈夫洛克（Havelock）吗？”

===Original===
Dick shook his head.
“He’s a pretty good man. His office is somewhere in Lincoln’s Inn Fields. You’ll find its exact position in the telephone directory. I met him at lunch and he asked me﻿—”

===Chinese===
迪克摇了摇头。
“他这人相当不错。他的事务所在林肯律师学院广场（Lincoln’s Inn Fields）一带。确切位置你在电话簿里查得到。我午饭时碰到他，他问我——”

===Original===
He paused, examining the younger man with a speculative eye.
“You’re the very man﻿—it is curious I did not think of you. He asked me if I could find him a reliable private detective, and I told him that such things did not exist outside the pages of fiction.”

===Chinese===
他顿了顿，用揣度的目光端详着这个年轻人。
“你就是最合适的人选——真奇怪，我先前竟没想到你。他问我能不能给他找一位可靠的私家侦探，我告诉他，这种人物只存在于小说的书页里。”

===Original===
“It doesn’t exist as far as I’m concerned,” smiled Dick. “The last thing in the world I want to do is start a detective agency.”

===Chinese===
“就我而言，这种人物根本不存在。”迪克微笑道，“天底下我最不想干的事，就是开侦探社。”

===Original===
“And you’re right, my boy,” said the commissioner. “I could never respect you if you did. As a matter of fact, you’re the very man for the job,” he went on, a little inconsistently. “Will you go along and see Havelock, and tell him I sent you? I’d like you to help him if you could. Although he isn’t a friend of mine, I know him and he’s a very pleasant fellow.”

===Chinese===
“你这话在理，孩子。”总监说，“你要真开了侦探社，我就再也敬不起你了。不过说实在的，这活儿还就非你莫属。”他接着说道，前后有点自相矛盾，“你去见见哈夫洛克，就说是我派你去的，好不好？要是帮得上，我希望你帮他一把。他虽然算不上我的朋友，但我们认识，他这人挺讨人喜欢。”

===Original===
“What is the job?” asked the young man, by no means enthralled at the prospect.

===Chinese===
“是什么差事？”年轻人问道，对这前景实在提不起兴致。

===Original===
“I don’t know,” was the reply. “It may be one that you couldn’t undertake. But I’d like you to see him﻿—I half promised him that I would recommend somebody. I have an idea that it is in connection with a client of his who is giving him a little trouble. You would greatly oblige me, Martin, if you saw this gentleman.”

===Chinese===
“我不知道。”对方答道，“也许是你承接不了的差事。可我希望你去见见他——我半答应过他，说要荐个人。我琢磨着，事情八成和他的一位委托人有关，那人正给他添点小麻烦。你要是肯见见这位先生，马丁，就是帮了我大忙。”

===Original===
The last thing in the world Dick Martin had in mind was the transference of his detective activities from Scotland Yard to the sphere of private agencies; but he had been something of a protégé of the third commissioner, and there was no reason in the world why he should not see the lawyer. He said as much.

===Chinese===
迪克·马丁天底下最不想干的，就是把侦探事业从苏格兰场挪到私家侦探社的地界上；可他多少算得上第三总监一手提携的后辈，天底下也没有任何理由不让他去见见这位律师。他便把这意思说了。

===Original===
“Good,” said the commissioner. “I’ll phone him this afternoon and tell him you’ll come along and see him. You may be able to help him.”
“I hope so, sir,” said Dick mendaciously.

===Chinese===
“好。”总监说，“今天下午我就打电话给他，说你要去见他。说不定你真能帮上他的忙。”
“但愿如此，长官。”迪克言不由衷地说。

## II / 二

===Original===
He pursued his leisurely way to the Bellingham Library, one of the institutions of London that is known only to a select few. No novel or volume of sparkling reminiscence has a place upon the shelves of this institution, founded a hundred years ago to provide scientists and littérateurs with an opportunity of consulting volumes which were unprocurable save at the British Museum. On the four floors which constituted the building, fat volumes of German philosophy, learned and, to the layman, unintelligible books on scientific phenomena, obscure treatises on almost every kind of uninteresting subject, stood shoulder to shoulder upon their sedate shelves.

===Chinese===
他不紧不慢地一路走到了贝灵厄姆图书馆——伦敦的机构里，只有寥寥少数人才知道的一处。这家机构创建于一百年前，为的是让科学家和文人们有机会查阅那些除了大英博物馆之外无处可觅的书籍；馆里的书架上没有一本小说，也没有一部妙语连珠的回忆录。在构成这座楼的四层楼面上，大部头的德国哲学著作、学究气十足而在外行看来不知所云的科学现象论著，以及几乎每一门枯燥学科的冷僻专论，都在静穆的书架上比肩而立。

===Original===
John Bellingham, who in the eighteenth century had founded this exchange of learning, had provided in the trust deeds that “two intelligent females, preferably in indigent circumstances,” should form part of the staff, and it was to one of these that Dick was conducted.

===Chinese===
十八世纪创建这座学术交流之所的约翰·贝灵厄姆（John Bellingham），在信托契据中规定馆内职员应包括“两名聪慧的女性，以家境清寒者为宜”，迪克被领去见的正是其中一位。

===Original===
In a small, high-ceilinged room, redolent of old leather, a girl sat at a table, engaged in filing index cards.

===Chinese===
在一间天花板很高、弥漫着旧皮革气味的小屋里，一位姑娘坐在桌前，正在给索引卡片归档。

===Original===
“I am from Scotland Yard,” Dick introduced himself. “I understand that some of your books have been stolen?”

===Chinese===
“我是苏格兰场来的。”迪克自我介绍道，“听说贵馆有些书被人偷了？”

===Original===
He was looking at the packed shelves as he spoke, for he was not interested in females, intelligent or stupid, indigent or wealthy. The only thing he noticed about her was that she wore black and that her hair was a golden-brown and was brushed into a fringe over her forehead. In a vague way he supposed that most girls had hair of golden-brown, and he had a dim idea that fringes were popular among working-class ladies.

===Chinese===
说话的当儿他望着塞得满满的书架，因为他对女性不感兴趣，聪明的也好，愚笨的也好，清寒的也好，阔绰的也好。他注意到她的只有一点：她一身黑衣，头发是金褐色的，在额前梳成一排刘海。他恍恍惚惚地以为大多数姑娘的头发都是金褐色，又模模糊糊地觉得刘海在劳动阶层的女士当中正时兴。

===Original===
“Yes,” she said quietly, “a book was stolen from this room whilst I was at luncheon. It was not very valuable﻿—a German volume written by Haeckel called *Generelle Morphologie*.”

===Chinese===
“是的。”她平静地说，“我吃午饭那会儿，这屋里丢了一本书。那书不算太贵重——是海克尔（Haeckel）写的一部德文书，叫《普通形态学》（*Generelle Morphologie*）。”

===Original===
She opened a drawer and took out an index card and laid it before him, and he read the words without being greatly enlightened.

===Chinese===
她拉开一个抽屉，取出一张索引卡，摆在他面前；他读了读上面的字，没有受到多少启发。

===Original===
“Who was here in your absence?” he asked.

“My assistant, a girl named Helder.”

===Chinese===
“你不在的时候，谁在这屋里？”他问。

“我的助手，一个叫赫尔德（Helder）的姑娘。

===Original===
“Did any of your subscribers come into this room during that time?”

“Several,” she replied. “I have their names, but most of them are above suspicion. The only visitor we had who is not a subscriber of the library was a gentleman named Stalletti, an Italian doctor, who called to make inquiries as to subscription.”

===Chinese===
“那段时间里，有没有哪位订户进过这间屋？”

“有好几位。”她答道，“名字我这里都有，不过他们大多无可怀疑。当天来访的人里，只有一位不是本馆的订户——一位名叫斯塔莱蒂（Stalletti）的先生，意大利医生，是来打听入订事宜的。”

===Original===
“He gave his name?” asked Dick.

“No,” said the girl to his surprise; “but Miss Helder recognized him; she had seen his portrait somewhere. I should have thought you would have remembered his name.”

===Chinese===
“他报了姓名？”迪克问。

“没有。”姑娘的答话让他颇为意外，“不过赫尔德小姐认出了他；她在哪儿见过他的画像。我原以为你会记得他的名字。”

===Original===
“Why on earth should I remember his name, my good girl?” asked Dick a little irritably.

“Why on earth shouldn’t you, my good man?” she demanded coolly, and at that moment Dick Martin was aware of her, in the sense that she emerged from the background against which his life moved and became a personality.

===Chinese===
“我到底凭什么该记得他的名字，我的好姑娘？”迪克有点恼火地问。

“你到底凭什么不该记得呢，我的好先生？”她冷冷地诘问。就在这一刻，迪克·马丁才真正留意到她——也就是说，她从他生活赖以活动的那幅背景中显现出来，成了一个活生生的人物。

===Original===
Her eyes were grey and set wide apart; her nose straight and small; the mouth was a little wide﻿—and she certainly had golden-brown hair.

===Chinese===
她的眼睛是灰色的，两眼分得很开；鼻子又直又小巧；嘴巴略宽了些——而那头发，确确实实是金褐色的。

===Original===
“I beg your pardon!” he laughed. “As a matter of fact”﻿—he had a trick of confidence which could be very deceptive﻿—“I’m not at all interested in this infernal robbery. I’m leaving the police force tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
“请您原谅！”他笑道。“说实在的”——他有种对人推心置腹的脾气，很能迷惑人——“这桩可恶的窃案我一点不感兴趣。我明天就离开警队了。”

===Original===
“There will be great joy amongst the criminal classes,” she said politely, and when he saw the light of laughter in her eyes, his heart went out to her.

===Chinese===
“犯罪阶层这下要皆大欢喜了。”她彬彬有礼地说。待他看见她眼中闪动的笑光，他的心便向她倾了过去。

===Original===
“You have a sense of humour,” he smiled.

“You mean by that, that I’ve a sense of *your* humour,” she answered quickly. “I have, or I should very much object to being called ‘my good girl’ even by an officer of the law”﻿—she looked at his card again﻿—“even with the rank of sub-inspector.”

===Chinese===
“你有幽默感。”他微笑道。

“你是想说，我领会得了*你*的那种幽默。”她伶俐地答道，“我确实领会得了，不然的话，哪怕对方是执法官员，人家唤我一声‘我的好姑娘’，我也是要大大见怪的”——她又看了一眼他的名片——“哪怕人家挂着副督察的衔。”

===Original===
There was a chair at his hand. Dick drew it out and sat down unbidden.

===Chinese===
他手边正有一把椅子。迪克把它拉出来，不等相让就坐下了。

===Original===
“I abase myself for my rudeness, and humbly beg information on the subject of Signor Stalletti. The name means no more to me than John Smith﻿—the favourite pseudonym of all gentlemen caught in the act of breaking through the pantry window in the middle of the night.”

===Chinese===
“我为方才的无礼自贬谢罪，并谦卑地恳请赐教斯塔莱蒂先生的种种。这名字于我，与约翰·史密斯并无二致——那是所有半夜里正撬开食品间窗户时被当场拿获的绅士们最钟爱的化名。”

===Original===
For a second she surveyed him gravely, her red lips pursed.

“And you’re a detective?” she said, in a hushed voice. “One of those almost human beings who protect us while we sleep!”

===Chinese===
她红唇微抿，正色打量了他一秒钟。

“而你就是个侦探？”她压低嗓音说，“就是那种趁我们酣睡时保护我们的、差一点儿就算得上人的生物之一！”

===Original===
He was helpless with laughter.

===Chinese===
他笑得没有还手之力。

===Original===
“I surrender!” He put up his hands. “And now, having put me in my place, which I admit is a pretty lowly one, perhaps you will pass across a little information about the purloined literature.”

===Chinese===
“我投降！”他举起双手。“如今你既已把我贬到了我该待的位置——我承认那位置相当低微——那么，关于那部被窃的文献，可否请你赏传一点儿情报过来。”

===Original===
“I’ve no information to pass across.” She leaned back in her chair, looking at him interestedly. “The book was here at two o’clock; it was not here at half past two﻿—there may be fingerprints on the shelf, but I doubt it, because we keep three charladies for the sole purpose of cleaning up fingerprints.”

===Chinese===
“我没有什么情报可赏传。”她往椅背上一靠，饶有兴味地打量着他。“两点钟的时候书还在；两点半就不见了——书架上也许留有指纹，不过我存疑，因为我们专门养着三个清洁女工，唯一的职责便是清除指纹。”

===Original===
“But who is Stalletti?”

She nodded slowly.

===Chinese===
“可斯塔莱蒂是什么人？”

她缓缓点了点头。

===Original===
“That was why I expressed a little wonder about your being a detective,” she said. “My assistant tells me that he is known to the police. Would you like to see his book?”

“Has he written a book?” he asked in genuine surprise.

===Chinese===
“方才我之所以对你这位侦探的身份表示过一点儿诧异，原因就在这里。”她说，“我的助手告诉我，此人是警方挂了号的人物。你想不想看看他的书？”

“他还写过书？”他问，当真吃了一惊。

===Original===
She got up, went out of the room and returned with a thin volume, plainly bound. He took the volume in his hand and read the title.

===Chinese===
她站起身，走出房去，回来时拿着一本薄薄的、装帧朴素的书。他把书拿在手里，念出书名。

===Original===
“*New Thoughts on Constructive Biology*, by Antonio Stalletti.”

===Chinese===
“《建设性生物学新论》（*New Thoughts on Constructive Biology*），安东尼奥·斯塔莱蒂（Antonio Stalletti）著。”

===Original===
Turning the closely printed leaves, broken almost at every page by diagrams and statistical tables, he asked:

“Why did he get into trouble with the police? I didn’t know that it was a criminal offence to write a book.”

===Chinese===
他翻动着排印得密密麻麻的书页——几乎每页都被图解和统计表打断——一边问：

“他怎么会跟警方结的梁子？我可不知道写书竟是刑事犯罪。”

===Original===
“It is,” she said emphatically; “but not invariably punished as such. I understand that the law took no exception to Mr. Stalletti being an author; and that his offence was in connection with vivisection or something equally horrid.”

===Chinese===
“写书确实是犯罪。”她斩钉截铁地说，“只是未必回回都照此惩办。据我所知，法律并不反对斯塔莱蒂先生当作家；他的罪过是在活体解剖上，或是别的什么同样骇人的事由。”

===Original===
“What is all this about?” He handed the book back to her.

“It is about human beings,” she said solemnly, “like you and me; and how much better and happier they would be if, instead of being mollycoddled﻿—I think that is the scientific term﻿—they were allowed to run wild in a wood and fed on a generous diet of nuts.”

===Chinese===
“这书究竟讲些什么？”他把书递还给她。

“讲的是人类，”她郑重其事地说，“就像你和我这样的人；讲的是倘若不由着他们娇生惯养——我想这是个科学术语——而是放他们到树林里撒野，喂他们分量充足的坚果，他们将会变得多么更好、多么幸福。”

===Original===
“Oh, vegetarian stuff!” said Slick contemptuously.

“Not exactly vegetarian. But perhaps you would like to become a subscriber and read it for yourself?”

===Chinese===
“哦，吃素那一套！”“滑头”轻蔑地说。

“倒不全是吃素。不过，你愿不愿意入个订户，亲自读上一读？”

===Original===
And then she dropped her tone of banter.

===Chinese===
随后她收起了打趣的腔调。

===Original===
“The truth is, Mr.﻿—er﻿—” she looked at his card again﻿—“Martin, we are really not worried about the loss of this book of Haeckel’s. It is already replaced, and if the secretary hadn’t been such a goop he wouldn’t have reported the matter to the police. And I beg of you”﻿—she raised a warning finger﻿—“if you meet our secretary that you will not repeat my opinion of him. Now please tell me something that will make my flesh creep. I’ve never met a detective before; I may never meet one again.”

===Chinese===
“实情是这样，先生——呃——”她又看了一眼他的名片——“马丁先生，海克尔这本书丢了，我们其实并不发愁。它已经补上了；要不是秘书那么个蠢货，这事本也不至于报给警察。还有，我恳求你”——她竖起一根手指以示警告——“倘若你遇上我们的秘书，千万别把我对他的这番评语学给他听。好了，现在请给我讲点儿能让我汗毛直竖的事吧。我从前从没见过侦探；往后兴许再也见不着了。”

===Original===
Dick put down the book and rose to his seventy-two inches.

“Madam,” he said, “I have not mustered courage to ask your name, I deserve all the roasting you have given me, but as you are strong, be merciful. Where does Stalletti live?”

===Chinese===
迪克放下书，直起他七十二英寸的身条。

“夫人，”他说，“我一直没敢鼓起勇气请教您的芳名，您方才那一顿奚落是我罪有应得；不过，您既占上风，还望高抬贵手。斯塔莱蒂住在哪儿？”

===Original===
She picked up the book and turned back the cover to a preface.

“Gallows Cottage. That sounds a little creepy doesn’t it? It is in Sussex.”

===Chinese===
她拿起那本书，翻开封面，翻到一篇序言。

“绞架小屋（Gallows Cottage）。这名字听着有点儿阴森森的，是不是？就在萨塞克斯（Sussex）。”

===Original===
“I can read that for myself,” he said, nettled, and she became instantly penitent.

“You see, we aren’t used to these exciting interludes, and a police visitation gets into one’s head. I really don’t think the book’s worth bothering about, but I suppose my word doesn’t go very far.”

===Chinese===
“这个我自己认得。”他说，有些着恼；她顿时悔过似的软了下来。

“您瞧，我们过不惯这种叫人兴奋的插曲，警察大驾光临，弄得人脑袋发昏。我真心觉得这本书犯不上兴师动众，不过我的话想必人微言轻。”

===Original===
“Was anybody here besides Stalletti?”

She showed him a list of four names.

===Chinese===
“除了斯塔莱蒂，还有谁来过？”

她给他看一张写着四个名字的单子。

===Original===
“Except Mr. Stalletti, I don’t think anybody is under suspicion. As a matter of fact, the other three people were severely historical, and biology wouldn’t interest them in the slightest degree. It could not have happened if I had been here, because I’m naturally rather observant.”

===Chinese===
“除斯塔莱蒂先生之外，我看谁也没有嫌疑。说实在的，另外三位都是极严谨的治史之人，生物学丝毫提不起他们的兴趣。倘若当时我在场，这事断乎发生不了，因为我天生就相当眼尖心细。”

===Original===
She stopped suddenly and looked at the desk. The book that had been lying there a few seconds before had disappeared.

===Chinese===
她忽然停住，望向书桌。几秒钟前还搁在那儿的那本书，已经不见了。

===Original===
“Did you take it?” she asked.

“Did you see me take it?” he challenged.

“I certainly didn’t. I could have sworn it was there a second ago.”

===Chinese===
“是不是你拿的？”她问。

“你瞧见我拿了吗？”他反诘道。

“当然没瞧见。刚才那一瞬它分明还在，我可以起誓。”

===Original===
He took it from under his coat and handed it to her.

“I like observant people,” he said.

===Chinese===
他从外套底下取出书来，递还给她。

“我喜欢眼尖的人。”他说。

===Original===
“But how did you do it?” She was mystified. “I had my hand on the book and I only took my eyes off for a second.”

===Chinese===
“可你是怎么得手的？”她如坠五里雾中。“我的手一直按在书上，眼睛不过挪开了一秒钟。”

===Original===
“One of these days I’ll come along and teach you,” he said with portentious gravity, and was in the street before he remembered that clever as he was, he had not succeeded in learning the name of this very capable young lady.

===Chinese===
“改天我过来教教你。”他说这话时郑重得煞有介事；直到人已到了街上他才想起，自己纵然手段高明，却终究没能探得这位非常能干的年轻姑娘的芳名。

===Original===
Sybil Lansdown walked to the window which commanded a view of the square and watched him till he was out of sight, a half smile on her lips and the light of triumph in her eyes. Her first inclination was to dislike him intensely; she hated self-satisfied men. And yet he wasn’t exactly that. She wondered if she would ever meet him again﻿—there were so few amusing people in the world, and she felt that﻿—she took up the card﻿—Sub-Inspector Richard Martin might be very amusing indeed.

===Chinese===
西比尔·兰斯当（Sybil Lansdown）走到能望见广场的窗前，目送他直到人影全无，唇边噙着半抹笑意，眼里闪着得胜的光。她起初一心想要狠狠地讨厌他；她憎恶自命不凡的男人。可他又算不上自命不凡。她不知道今生还能不能再见着他——世上有趣的人实在太少了，而她觉得——她拿起那张名片——副督察理查德·马丁这人，说不定当真非常有趣呢。

## III / 三

===Original===
Dick was piqued to the extent of wishing to renew the encounter, and there was only one excuse for that. He went to the garage near his flat, took out his dingy Buick and drove down to Gallows Hill. It was not an easy quest, because Gallows Hill is not marked on the map and only had a local significance; and it was not until he was on the edge of Selford Manor that he learnt from a road-mender that the cottage was on the main road and that he had come about ten miles out of his way.

It was late in the afternoon when he drew abreast of the broken wall and hanging gate behind which was the habitation of Dr. Stalletti. The weed-grown drive turned abruptly to reveal a mean-looking house, which he thought was glorified by the name of cottage. So many of his friends had “cottages” which were mansions, and “little places” which were very little indeed, when he had expected to find a more lordly dwelling.
===Chinese===
迪克心绪被撩拨得难平，竟起了再见那人一面的念头，而要再见面的借口只有一个。他到公寓附近的车库，取出那辆灰扑扑的别克车，一路开往绞架山（Gallows Hill）。这一趟可不好找，因为绞架山在地图上并无标注，只是个当地人才知道的地名；直到开到塞尔福德庄园（Selford Manor）地界边沿，他才从一个修路工嘴里打听到，那座小屋就在大路边上，而他已多绕了约莫十英里的冤枉路。

等他把车开得与那截断墙、那扇摇摇欲坠的大门并齐，已是午后迟暮时分——门后便是斯塔莱蒂医生（Dr. Stalletti）的住处。杂草丛生的车道陡然一拐，露出一座面目寒酸的房子，他觉得“小屋”这名称实在是抬举了它。他的朋友当中，有多少人的“小屋”其实是深宅大院，又有多少人的“小地方”当真小得可怜——而他原本还指望能寻见一处更有气派的宅邸呢。

===Original===
There was no bell, and he knocked at the weather-stained door for five minutes before he had an answer. And then he heard a shuffling of feet on bare boards, the clang of a chain being removed, and the door opened a few inches.

Accustomed as he was to unusual spectacles, he gaped at the man who was revealed in the space between door and lintel. A long, yellow face, deeply lined and crisscrossed with innumerable lines till it looked like an ancient yellow apple; a black beard that half-covered its owner’s waistcoat; a greasy skullcap; a pair of black, malignant eyes blinking at him﻿—these were his first impressions.
===Chinese===
门上没有门铃，他在那扇饱经风雨的门前敲了足足五分钟，才得到回应。接着他听见光秃秃的木地板上响起趿沓的脚步声，铁链卸下时的当啷一响，门开了一条几寸宽的缝。

他虽见惯了各色奇人异相，此刻却不由得对着门框间露出身影的那个人张口结舌。一张狭长的黄脸，皱纹深刻、纵横交错，密得像一只陈年的黄苹果；一部黑胡须半掩着主人的马甲；一顶油腻腻的瓜皮帽；一对黑幽幽、恶狠狠的眼睛朝他直眨——这些便是他的第一印象。

===Original===
“Dr. Stalletti?” he asked.

“That is my name.” The voice was harsh, with just a suggestion of a foreign accent. “Did you wish to speak with me? Yes? That is extraordinary. I do not receive visitors.”
===Chinese===
“斯塔莱蒂医生？”他问。

“正是我的名字。”那嗓音生硬，隐约带一丝外国腔。“你想同我说话？是吗？真是稀奇。我是不见客的。”

===Original===
He seemed in some hesitation as to what he should do, and then he turned his head and spoke to somebody over his shoulder, and in doing so revealed to the detective a young, rosy, and round-faced man, very newly and smartly dressed. At the sight of Dick the man stepped back quickly out of sight.

“Good morning, Thomas,” said Dick Martin politely. “This is an unexpected pleasure.” The bearded man growled something and opened the door wide.
===Chinese===
他一时踌躇着不知如何是好，随后扭过头，朝肩后什么人说了句话；这一扭头，便让侦探望见了一个红光满面的圆脸青年，一身簇新时兴的打扮。那青年一看见迪克，赶忙缩身退出了视线。

“早上好，托马斯（Thomas）。”迪克·马丁客客气气地说，“想不到在这儿遇上你，真是个意外之喜。”大胡子低声咕哝了句什么，把门敞开了。

===Original===
Tommy Cawler was indeed a sight for sore eyes. Dick Martin had seen him in many circumstances, but never so beautifully and perfectly arrayed. His linen was speckless; his clothes were the product of a West End tailor.

“Good morning, Mr. Martin.” Tommy was in no sense abashed. “I just happened to call round to see my old friend Stalletti.”

Dick gazed at him admiringly.
===Chinese===
汤米·考勒（Tommy Cawler）这一身行头，当真叫人眼前一亮。迪克·马丁在各种场合都见过他，却从没见他穿得这样漂亮齐整过。他的衬衣雪白无瑕，衣裳出自伦敦西区（West End）的裁缝之手。

“早上好，马丁先生。”汤米丝毫没有不好意思。“我不过是顺道过来，瞧瞧我的老朋友斯塔莱蒂。”

迪克满眼赞叹地打量着他。

===Original===
“You simply ooze prosperity! What is the game now, Tommy?”

Tommy closed his eyes, a picture of patience and resignation.

“I’ve got a good job now, Mr. Martin﻿—straight as a die! No more trouble for me, thank you. Well, I’ll be saying goodbye, doctor.”
===Chinese===
“你这简直是浑身往外冒财气！如今又玩上什么把戏了，汤米？”

汤米闭上眼睛，一副坚忍克己、听天由命的神气。

“我如今有份好差事，马丁先生——规规矩矩，一点不掺假！我再不惹麻烦了，谢谢您惦记。好啦，我就此告辞，医生。”

===Original===
He shook hands a little too vigorously with the bearded man and stepped past him and down the steps.

“Wait a moment, Tommy. I’d like to have a few words with you. Can you spare me a moment whilst I see Dr. Stalletti?”

The man hesitated, shot a furtive glance at the bearded figure in the doorway.
===Chinese===
他跟大胡子握手握得未免过分用力，随即侧身挤过对方，走下台阶。

“等一等，汤米。我想跟你说几句话。我进去见斯塔莱蒂医生的这会儿，你能腾出片刻等我吗？”

那人迟疑了一下，朝门口大胡子的身影偷偷溜了一眼。

===Original===
“All right,” he said ungraciously. “But don’t be long, I’ve got an engagement. Thank you for the medicine, doctor,” he added loudly.

Dick was not deceived by so transparent a bluff.

He followed the doctor into the hall. Farther the strange man did not invite him.
===Chinese===
“行吧。”他不情愿地说，“可别耽搁太久，我还有个约会。”他又大声添上一句：“多谢您的药，医生。”

这样露骨的障眼法骗不过迪克。

他跟着医生进了门厅。再往里去，那怪人可就没请他了。

===Original===
“You are police, yes?” he said, when Dick produced his card. “How extraordinary and bizarre! To me the police have not come for a long time﻿—such trouble for a man because he experiments for science on a leetle dog! Such a fuss and nonsense! Now you ask me﻿—what?”

In a few words Dick explained his errand, and to his amazement the strange man answered immediately:
===Chinese===
“你是警察，是吗？”迪克亮出名片时，他说道。“多么稀奇、多么古怪！警察已经很久没有上我这儿来了——一个人为了科学在一只小小狗身上做做实验，就给他招来这样的麻烦！真是小题大做、莫名其妙！现在你要问我——什么？”

迪克三言两语讲明来意，岂料那怪人当即答道：

===Original===
“Yes, the book, I have it! It was on the shelf. I needed it, so I took it!”

“But, my good man,” said the staggered detective, “you’re not allowed to walk off with other people’s property because you want it!”
===Chinese===
“是了，那本书，在我这儿！它当时就在架子上。我用得着，就把它拿走了！”

“可是，我的好先生，”侦探愕然道，“总不能因为自己想用，就把别人的东西顺手拿走哇！”

===Original===
“It is a library. It is for lending, is it not? I desired to borrow, so I took it with me. There was no concealment. I placed it under my arm, I lifted my hat to the young signora, and that was all. Now I have finished with it and it may go back. Haeckel is a fool; his conclusions are absurd, his theories extraordinary and bizarre.” (Evidently this was a favourite phrase of his.) “To you they would seem very dull and commonplace, but to me﻿—” He shrugged his shoulders and uttered a little cackle of sound which Dick gathered was intended to be laughter.
===Chinese===
“那是个图书馆。图书馆就是出借书籍的地方，不是吗？我想借，就带走了。没有任何隐瞒。我把它夹在胳膊底下，向那位年轻小姐举了举帽子，仅此而已。如今我用完了，它可以物归原主了。海克尔（Haeckel）是个蠢货；他的结论荒谬，他的理论稀奇又古怪。”（这显然是他的口头禅。）“在你看来，那些东西想必枯燥乏味、平淡无奇，可在我——”他耸了耸肩，发出一小串咯咯的怪声，迪克揣摩那大概是笑。

===Original===
The detective delivered a little lecture on the systems of loaning libraries, and with the book under his arm went out to rejoin the waiting Mr. Cawler. He had at least an excuse for returning to the library, he thought with satisfaction.

“Now, Cawler”﻿—he began without superfluous preliminaries and his voice was peremptory﻿—“I want to know something about you. Is Stalletti a friend of yours?”
===Chinese===
侦探就流通图书馆的借阅制度小小训诫了一通，然后夹起那本书，出门去找候在外面的考勒先生。他总算有了个再上图书馆去的借口，想到这里，他心下颇为满意。

“喂，考勒，”他开门见山，语气不容分说，“我要了解一点你的情况。斯塔莱蒂是你的朋友吗？”

===Original===
“He’s my doctor,” said the man coolly.

He had a merry blue eye, and he was one of the few people who had passed through his hands for whom Dick had a genuine liking. Tommy Cawler had been a notorious “knocker-off” of motorcars, and a “knocker-off” is one who, finding an unattended machine, steps blithely into the driver’s seat and is gone before the owner misses his machine. Tommy’s two convictions had both been due to the unremitting inquiries of the man who now questioned him.
===Chinese===
“他是我的医生。”那人淡淡地说。

他生着一双快活的蓝眼睛；在迪克手里走过一遭的各色人等之中，他是少数几个让迪克真心喜欢的人。汤米·考勒从前是个臭名昭著的“顺车贼”——所谓“顺车贼”，就是撞见没人看管的汽车，便大大咧咧坐进驾驶座，趁车主还没发觉车没了，人已经扬长而去。汤米两次被判刑，都是拜眼前这个审问他的人锲而不舍的追查所赐。

===Original===
“I’ve got a regular job; I’m chauffeur to Mr. Bertram Cody,” said Tom virtuously. “I’m that honest now, I wouldn’t touch anything crook, not to save my life.”

“Where does Mr. Cody live when he’s at home?” asked Dick, unconvinced.

“Weald House. It is only a mile from here; you can step over and ask if you like.”
===Chinese===
“我如今有份正正经经的差事；我给贝特伦·科迪先生（Mr. Bertram Cody）当私人司机。”汤姆一脸正气地说，“我这会儿老实得很，歪门邪道一概不沾，要我命也不沾。”

“科迪先生家住哪儿？”迪克问，心里全不信。

“威尔德宅邸（Weald House）。离这儿才一英里；您乐意的话，尽管过去问一问。”

===Original===
“Does he know about your﻿—sad past?” Dick questioned delicately.

“He does; I told him everything. He says I am the best chauffeur he ever had.”

Dick examined the man carefully.
===Chinese===
“他知道不知道你那段——伤心的往事？”迪克委婉地问道。

“知道，我原原本本都告诉他了。他说我是他雇过的司机里最好的一个。”

迪克把这个人细细打量了一番。

===Original===
“Is this er﻿—er﻿—uniform that your employer prefers?”

“I’m going on holiday, to tell you the truth,” said Mr. Cawler. “The governor is pretty good about holidays. Here’s the address if you want it.”

He took an envelope from his pocket addressed to himself, “c/o Bertram Cody, Esq., Weald House, South Weald, Sussex.”
===Chinese===
“这身呃——呃——制服，是你的雇主所中意的式样吗？”

“不瞒您说，我这是要去度假。”考勒先生说，“东家在放假上头挺大方的。地址在这儿，您要不要？”

他从口袋里掏出一个信封，上面写着寄给他本人的地址：“萨塞克斯郡南威尔德，威尔德宅邸，贝特伦·科迪先生转交”（c/o Bertram Cody, Esq., Weald House, South Weald, Sussex）。

===Original===
“They treat me like a lord,” he said, not without truth. “And a more perfect lady and gentleman than Mr. and Mrs. Cody you’d never hope to see.”

“Fine,” said the sceptical Richard. “Forgive these embarrassing questions, Tommy, but in my bright lexicon there is no such word as ‘reform.’ ”

“I don’t know your friend, but you’ve got it wrong,” said Tommy hazily.
===Chinese===
“他们待我像待老爷一样。”他说，这话倒也不假。“像科迪先生和科迪太太那样十全十美的绅士淑女，你打着灯笼也别想再见着第二对。”

“好得很。”将信将疑的理查德说，“原谅我提这些叫人难堪的问题，汤米，不过在我那部光鲜的词典里，可没有‘改过自新’这个词。”

“我不认识您那位朋友，反正您这话弄错了。”汤米含含糊糊地说。

===Original===
Martin offered him a lift, but this was declined, and the detective went back alone to London, and, to his annoyance, arrived at the library half an hour after the girl had left.

It was too late, he thought, to see Mr. Havelock of Lincoln’s Inn Fields, and in point of fact the recollection of that engagement brought with it a feeling of discomfort. His plans were already made. He intended spending a month in Germany before he returned to the work which he had promised himself: a volume on *Thieves and Their Methods*, which he thought would pleasantly occupy the next year.

Dick, without being extremely wealthy, was in a very comfortable position. Sneed had spoken of a six-figure legacy, and was nearly right, although the figures were dollars, for his uncle had been a successful cattle farmer of Alberta. Mainly he was leaving the police force because he was nearing promotion, and felt it unfair to stand in the way of other men who were more in need of rank than himself. Police work amused him. It was his hobby and occupation, and he did not care to contemplate what life would be without that interest.
===Chinese===
马丁提出捎他一程，被谢绝了，侦探便独自驱车回伦敦；让他恼火的是，赶到图书馆时，姑娘已经走了半个钟头。

他想，这时候再去见林肯律师学院广场的哈夫洛克先生未免太晚；说实话，一想起那桩约见，他心里反倒泛起一阵不自在。他的计划早已定妥：先去德国盘桓一个月，再回头投入他给自己许下的事业——写一部《窃贼及其手法》（*Thieves and Their Methods*），他指望这部书能悠悠快快地打发掉来年一整年。

迪克虽算不上巨富，境况却十分宽裕。斯尼德曾说过一笔六位数的遗产，这话也没大错，只不过那数字是美元——他的叔叔本是艾伯塔（Alberta）一位发家的养牛场主。他决意离开警队，主要是因为眼看就要升职，觉得再占着位子、挡了那些比他更需要警衔的人的路，未免不公。警务于他是一桩乐事，既是嗜好，又是营生，他不肯去想，缺了这份兴味，生活将成个什么样子。

===Original===
He had turned to go into his flat when he heard a voice hail him, and he turned to see the man whom he had released that morning crossing the road in some haste. Ordinarily, Lew Pheeney was the coolest of men, but now he was almost incoherent.

“Can I see you, Slick?” he asked, a quiver in his voice, which Dick did not remember having heard before.
===Chinese===
他正要转身走进公寓，忽听有人喊他；回头一看，只见那天早上他亲手放走的那个人正急匆匆地穿过马路。平日里，卢·菲尼是天底下最沉得住气的人，可这会儿他连话都说不连贯了。

“能跟您说几句话吗，滑头？”他问，嗓音里带着一丝颤抖——迪克不记得从前听他这样过。

===Original===
“Surely you can see me. Why? Is anything wrong?”

“I don’t know.” The man looked up and down the street nervously. “I’m being trailed.”

“Not by the police﻿—that I can swear,” said Dick.
===Chinese===
“跟我说话有什么不能的。怎么啦？出了什么事？”

“我不知道。”那人紧张地朝街头两头张望。“有人盯上我了。”

“盯你的不是警察——这个我可以打包票。”迪克说。

===Original===
“Police!” said the man impatiently. “Do you think that would worry me? No, it’s the fellow﻿—I spoke to you about. There’s something wrong in that business. Slick, I kept one thing from you. While I was working I saw this guy slip a gun out of his hip and drop it into his overcoat pocket. He stood holding it all the time I was working, and it struck me then that, if I’d got that door open, there’d have been no chance of my ever touching the thousand. Halfway through I said I wanted to go out, and, once outside, I bolted. There was something that chased me﻿—God knows what it was; a sort of animal. And I hadn’t got a gun﻿—I never carry one in this country, because a judge piles it on if you’re caught with a barker in your pocket.”
===Chinese===
“警察！”那人不耐烦地说，“你以为那能叫我犯愁？不，是那家伙——我跟你说过的那个。那桩买卖里有古怪。滑头，有件事我瞒了你。我干活的时候，瞧见那家伙从腰里拔出一支枪，塞进了大衣口袋。我干活的整段工夫，他都握着枪站在那儿。我当时就琢磨明白了：真让我把那扇门打开，那一千镑只怕一个子儿也轮不到我拿。干到一半，我说要出去一趟，一出了门，我撒腿就跑。有东西追我——天知道那是什么；像头什么畜生似的。可我手里没枪——在这个国家我从不带枪，因为要是叫人逮着兜里揣着家伙，法官非得罪上加罪不可。”

===Original===
All the time they had been speaking they were passing through the vestibule and up the stairs to Slick’s flat, and, without invitation, the burglar followed him into the apartment.

He led the man into his study and shut the door.

“Now, Lew, let me hear the truth﻿—what was the work you were doing on Tuesday night?”
===Chinese===
两人说这番话的工夫，一路穿过门厅、上了楼，来到滑头的公寓；窃贼也不等人相让，跟着便进了屋。

他把这人领进书房，关上了门。

“好了，卢，让我听听实话——星期二晚上你干的是什么活儿？”

===Original===
Lew looked round the room, out of the window, everywhere except at Dick. Then:

“I was trying to open a dead man’s tomb!” he said in a low voice.
===Chinese===
卢的目光在屋里四下逡巡，望向窗外，哪儿都看了，就是不看迪克。然后：

“我那是想打开一座死人的坟墓！”他低声说。

## IV / 四

===Original===

There was a silence of a minute. Dick looked at the man, hardly believing his ears.

===Chinese===

沉默了约莫一分钟。迪克望着这个人，简直不敢相信自己的耳朵。

===Original===

“Trying to open a dead man’s tomb?” he repeated. “Now sit down and tell me all about it, Lew.”

“I can’t﻿—yet. I’m scared,” said the other doggedly. “This man is hell, and I’d as soon face the devil as go through another night like I had on Tuesday.”

===Chinese===

“想撬开一个死人的坟墓？”他重复了一遍，“好了，坐下吧，把事情一五一十讲给我听，卢。”

“我还说不得——暂时说不得。我害怕呀。”对方执拗地说，“那人简直是个恶魔，我宁可去直面魔鬼，也不愿再熬一个星期二那样的夜晚。”

===Original===

“Who is the man?”

“I won’t tell you that,” said the other sullenly. “I might at the end, but I won’t tell you now. If I can find a quiet place I’m going to write it all out, and have it on paper in case﻿—anything happens to me.”

===Chinese===

“那人是谁？”

“这我可不会告诉你。”对方闷闷地说，“到最后兴许会说，现在是不说的。我要是能寻个清静地方，就把这一切原原本本写下来，白纸黑字留在纸上，万一——我出了什么事。”

===Original===

He was obviously labouring under a sense of unusual excitement, and Dick, who had known him for many years, both in England and in Canada, was amazed to see this usually phlegmatic man in such a condition of nerves.

He refused to take the dinner that the old housekeeper served, contenting himself with a whisky and soda, and Dick Martin thought it wise not to attempt to question him any further.

===Chinese===

他显然正被一阵异乎寻常的激动折磨着；迪克在英国也好、加拿大也好，同他相识多年，眼见这个平日处变不惊的人落得这般神经紧张，不由得暗暗称奇。

老管家端上晚饭，他不肯动箸，只喝了杯威士忌兑苏打便算了；迪克·马丁觉得，还是别再往下盘问的为妙。

===Original===

“Why don’t you stay here tonight and write your story? I won’t ask you for it, but you’ll be as safe here as anywhere.”

===Chinese===

“今晚你何不就待在这儿，把你的事写下来？我不会跟你讨看，再说你在这儿和在任何地方一样安全。”

===Original===

That idea seemed already to have occurred to the man, for he obeyed instantly, and Dick gathered that he had such a scheme in his mind. Dinner was nearly through when the detective was called away to the phone.

===Chinese===

这个主意那人心目中似乎早就有了，因为他二话不说便依了；迪克这才咂摸出来，他原就存着这份打算。晚饭将近用完时，侦探被人叫去接电话。

===Original===

“Is that Mr. Martin?”

The voice was that of a stranger.

“Yes,” replied Dick.

===Chinese===

“是马丁先生吗？”

说话的是一个陌生的嗓音。

“是我。”迪克答道。

===Original===

“I am Mr. Havelock. The Commissioner sent me a message this evening, and I was expecting you to call at my office. I wonder if you could see me tonight?”

There was anxiety and urgency in the tone.

===Chinese===

“我是哈夫洛克。总监今晚给我捎了个信，我本还指望你到我事务所来一趟。不知今晚你可愿见我一面？”

语气里透着焦灼，还带着急迫。

===Original===

“Why, surely,” said Dick. “Where are you living?”

“907, Acacia Road, St. John’s Wood. I am very near to you; a taxi would get you here in five minutes. Have you dined? I was afraid you had. Will you come up to coffee in about a quarter of an hour?”

===Chinese===

“哦，那自然。”迪克说，“您住在哪儿？”

“圣约翰伍德（St. John's Wood），阿卡西亚路（Acacia Road）907号。我离你很近，坐出租车五分钟就到。你用过晚饭没有？我倒怕你是用过了。一刻钟后上我这儿来喝杯咖啡，可好？”

===Original===

Dick Martin had agreed before he realized that his guest and his strange story had to be considered.

The startling announcement of Lew Pheeney had changed his plans. Yet it might be advisable to leave the man to write his story. He called his housekeeper aside and dismissed her for the night. Pheeney, alone in the flat, might write his story without interruption.

===Chinese===

迪克·马丁应承下来之后，才想起家里那位客人，还有他那离奇的故事，不能不掂量掂量。

卢·菲尼那句耸人听闻的招认已打乱了他的计划。不过，把这人撂下安心写他的故事，倒也不失为稳妥之策。他把老管家唤到一旁，打发她回去歇息。菲尼独自留在公寓里，正好不受打搅地写他的东西。

===Original===

The man readily agreed to his suggestion, seemed, in fact, relieved at the prospect of being alone, and a quarter of an hour later Mr. Martin was ringing the bell of an imposing house that stood in its acre of garden in the best part of St. Johns Wood. An elderly butler took his stick and hat and conducted him into a long dining-room, furnished with quiet taste. Evidently Mr. Havelock was something of a connoisseur, for of the four pictures that hung on the wall, Dick accurately placed one as being by Corot, and the big portrait over the carved mantelpiece was undoubtedly a Rembrandt.

===Chinese===

那人一听这提议便满口应承，看那样子，能得个独处的机会倒像是松了口气。一刻钟之后，马丁先生已站在圣约翰伍德最上等地段一座气派宅邸的门首按铃，宅子连同占地一英亩的花园自成一片天地。一位上了年纪的管家接过他的手杖和帽子，引他走进一间陈设清雅的长餐厅。哈夫洛克先生显然颇有几分鉴赏家的眼力：壁上挂着四幅画，迪克一眼便断定其中一幅出自柯罗（Corot）手笔，雕花壁炉架上方那幅大肖像，则毫无疑问是伦勃朗（Rembrandt）。

===Original===

The lawyer was dining in solitary state at the end of a long, polished table. A glass of red wine stood at his elbow, a long, thin cigar was between his teeth. He was a man between fifty and sixty, tall and rather thin. He had the brow and jaw of a fighter, and his iron-grey side-whiskers gave him a certain ferocious appearance. Dick liked him, for the eyes behind his horn-rimmed spectacles were very attractive.

===Chinese===

律师正独自一人在一张擦得锃亮的长桌尽头用餐，俨然一派威仪。肘边搁着一杯红葡萄酒，齿间衔着一支细长的雪茄。他五十到六十之间年纪，高而偏瘦，生就一副斗士的额头与下颚，铁灰色的连鬓络腮胡子又给他添了几分狰狞气象。迪克却对他颇有好感，因为角质框眼镜后面的那双眼睛，十分惹人喜爱。

===Original===

“Mr. Martin, eh?” He half rose and offered his firm, thin hand. “Sit down. What will you drink? I have a port here that was laid down for princes. Walters, give Mr. Martin a glass.”

===Chinese===

“马丁先生，嗯？”他半抬起身，伸出干瘦而有力的手。“请坐。喝点什么？我这儿有一款波特酒，当年窖下时原是备着款待王公的。沃尔特斯（Walters），给马丁先生斟一杯。”

===Original===

He leaned back in his chair, his lips pursed, and regarded the young man fixedly.

“So you’re a detective, eh?” It sounded reminiscent of an experience he had had that morning, and Dick grinned. “The commissioner says you’re leaving the police force tomorrow, and that you want a hobby. By heavens, I’ll furnish you a hobby that’ll save me a lot of sleepless nights! Walters, serve Mr. Martin and clear out. And I am not to be interrupted. Switch off the phone; I’m not at home to anybody, however important.”

===Chinese===

他往椅背上一靠，抿着嘴唇，定睛打量这个年轻人。

“这么说，你是个侦探，嗯？”这话听着叫他想起当天早晨的一段遭遇，迪克咧嘴笑了。“总监说你明天就要脱离警队，还说你正想寻个消遣。老天在上，我给你寻的这桩消遣，保管能省下我许多个不眠之夜！沃尔特斯，伺候马丁先生用过就都退下。我不见外客，不许打搅。把电话关掉——天大的事，就说我不在家。”

===Original===

When the door had closed behind the butler, Mr. Havelock rose and began a restless pacing of the room. He had a quick, abrupt, almost offensively brusque manner, jerking out his sentences accusatively.

===Chinese===

管家退出、房门合拢之后，哈夫洛克先生便站起身来，焦躁地在屋里踱来踱去。他举止急促生硬，唐突得近乎无礼，一句句话像蹦豆子似的往外迸，字字带着诘问的火气。

===Original===

“I’m a lawyer﻿—you probably know my name, though I’ve never been in a police court in my life. I’m very seldom in any court of law. I deal with companies and estates, and I’m trustee for half a dozen, or maybe a dozen, various charities. I’m the trustee of the Selford estate.” He said this with a certain emphasis, as though he thought that Dick would understand the peculiar significance of this. “I’m the trustee of the Selford estate,” he said again, “and I wish to heaven I wasn’t. Old Lord Selford﻿—not that he was old, except in sin and iniquity, but the late Lord Selford, let me say﻿—left me the sole executor of his property and guardian of his wretched child. The late Lord Selford was a very unpleasant, bad-tempered man, half mad, as most of the Selfords have been for generations. Do you know Selford Manor?”

===Chinese===

“我是个律师——我的名字你多半听说过，虽然我这辈子没进过治安法庭，旁的法庭也难得踏足。我做的是公司和产业方面的业务，还兼着六家、兴许一打各类慈善机构的受托人。我是塞尔福德（Selford）产业的受托人。”他说这一句时特意加重了语气，仿佛认为迪克自会掂量出这话的特别分量。“我是塞尔福德产业的受托人，”他又说了一遍，“我巴不得老天爷当初没把这差事派给我。老塞尔福德勋爵——他也并非真的老，只是论作孽使坏堪称老资格；这么说吧，已故的塞尔福德勋爵——把产业的唯一遗嘱执行人一职丢给了我，连他那可怜孩子的监护之责也一并撂在我肩上。这位已故的塞尔福德勋爵脾气极坏，是个顶讨人嫌的人物，疯了一半——塞尔福德家世世代代大抵都是这路货色。你可知道塞尔福德庄园（Selford Manor）？”

===Original===

Dick smiled.

“Curiously enough, I was on the edge of it today. I didn’t know there was such a place until this afternoon, and I had no such idea there was a Lord Selford﻿—does he live there?”

===Chinese===

迪克莞尔一笑。

“说来也巧，我今天刚打那庄园边上经过。到今天下午为止，我还不知道有这么个地方，也压根没想到世上还有位塞尔福德勋爵——他就住在那儿么？”

===Original===

“He doesn’t.” Havelock snapped the words, his eyes gleaming fiercely from behind his glasses. “I wish to God he did! He lives nowhere. That is to say, he lives nowhere longer than two or three days together. He is a nomad of nomads; his father in his youth was something of the same nature. Pierce﻿—that is his family name, by the way, and he has always been called Pierce﻿—has spent the last ten years wandering from town to town, from country to country, drawing heavily upon his revenue, as he can well afford to do because it is a large one, and returning to England only at the rarest intervals. I haven’t seen him for four years.” He said this slowly.

===Chinese===

“他不住那儿。”哈夫洛克把这几个字咬得斩钉截铁，眼镜后面双眼凶光毕露。“我倒求上天让他住那儿呢！他哪儿也不住。这么说吧，天底下没有一个地方他能连住两三天以上。他是个天字第一号的流浪汉；他父亲年轻时也有几分这样的秉性。皮尔斯（Pierce）——顺便说一句，这是他的家姓，而且大家向来只叫他皮尔斯——这十年间逛了一城又一城，走了一国又一国，大手大脚地支取他的进项——他那份进项着实丰厚，也禁得起他这般花销——难得回英国一遭。我已经四年没见过他了。”这几句话，他说得很慢。

===Original===

“I’ll give you his history, Mr. Martin, so that you will understand it better,” he went on. “When Selford died, Pierce was six. He had no mother, and, curiously enough, no near relations. Selford was an only child, and his wife was also in that position, so that there were no uncles and aunts to whom I could have handed over my responsibility. The boy was delicate, as I found when I sent him to a preparatory school at the age of eight, expecting to be rid of the poor little beggar, but not a day passed that he didn’t send me a note asking to be taken away. Eventually I found a private tutor for him, and he got some sort of education. It was not good enough to enable him to pass the Little Go﻿—that is the entrance examination to Cambridge﻿—and I sent him abroad with his tutor to travel. I wish to heaven I hadn’t! For the travel bug bit deep into his soul, and he’s been moving ever since. Four years ago he came to me in London. He was then on his way to America, where he was studying economic conditions. He had a wild idea of writing a book﻿—one of the delusions from which most people suffer is that other people are interested in their recollections.”

===Chinese===

“我把他这段来历讲给你听，马丁先生，你也好把事情看得更明白。”他接着说，“老塞尔福德去世时，皮尔斯才六岁。他没了母亲，说来也怪，近亲也一个没有。塞尔福德是独子，他妻子也是独女，因此我连个可以移交责任的叔伯姑姨都寻不出。这孩子身子单薄——我送他上预备学校那年他八岁，本指望就此脱开这可怜的小家伙，谁知他没有一天不捎张字条来求我把他领回去。到头来我给他延请了一位家庭教师，他总算受了些教育。可那点教育还不足以叫他通过‘小考’（Little Go）——也就是剑桥大学的入学考试——我便打发他随家庭教师出国游历去了。我真悔不该多此一举！游历这条毒虫深深刻进了他的魂里，打那以后他就再没停过脚。四年前他来伦敦寻我，那时他正要取道赴美，说是考察经济状况。他有个狂想的念头，要写一部书——世人多半都害着一种错觉，总以为旁人对自己的回忆录津津有味。”

===Original===

Dick flushed guiltily, but the lawyer went on, without apparently noticing his embarrassment.

===Chinese===

迪克心虚地红了脸，律师却径自往下说，像是全然不曾留意他的窘态。

===Original===

“Now I’m worried about this boy. From time to time demands come through to me for money, and from time to time I cable him very respectable sums﻿—which, of course, he is entitled to receive, for he is now twenty-four.”

===Chinese===

“如今我正为这孩子悬着心。他隔三差五便递话来支钱，我也隔三差五电汇给他一笔笔数目可观的款项——这自然是他名分上应得的，他如今已二十四岁了。”

===Original===

“His financial position﻿—” began Dick.

“Perfectly sound, perfectly sound,” said Mr. Havelock impressively. “That isn’t the question at all. What is worrying me is, the boy being so long out of my sight. Anything may happen to him; he may have fallen into the worst possible hands.” He hesitated, and added: “And I feel that I should get in touch with him﻿—not directly, but through a third person. In other words, I want you to go to America next week, and, without saying that you came from me, or that I sent you, get acquainted with Lord Selford﻿—he travels, by the way, as Mr. John Pierce. He is a very quick mover, and you’ll have to make careful inquiries as to where he has gone, because I cannot promise that I can keep you as well informed of his movements as I should like. If, in your absence, I have a cable from him, I will, of course, transmit it to you. I want you to find Pierce, but in no circumstances are you to acquaint the police of America that you are following him, or that there is anything suspicious in his movements. All that I want to know is, Has he contracted any undesirable alliance, is he a free agent, is the money I send to him being employed for his own benefit? He tells me, by the way, that he has bought a number of shares in industrial concerns in various parts of the world, and some of these shares are in my possession. A great number, however, I cannot account for, and he has replied to my inquiries by telling me that they are safely deposited with a South African banking corporation. The reason I ask you to keep this matter entirely to yourself is because, you will understand, I can’t have him embarrassed by the attentions of the local authorities. And most earnestly I am desirous that he should not know I sent you. Now, Mr. Martin, how does the idea appeal to you?”

===Chinese===

“他的财务状况——”迪克刚一开腔。

“好端端的，好端端的。”哈夫洛克先生一字一顿地说，“那根本不是问题的要害。叫我悬心的是，这孩子太长久不在我的眼皮底下。什么祸事都轮得上他沾边；说不定他已经落进顶顶糟糕的手掌心里了。”他顿了顿，又补上一句，“我寻思着，该同他通上气——不亲自出面，而是经由第三者。换句话说，我要你下星期去一趟美国，在不透露你从我这儿来、也不提是我差遣的前提下，设法结识塞尔福德勋爵——对了，他出门在外用的名号是约翰·皮尔斯（John Pierce）先生。他行踪飘忽，你得仔细打听他到了何处，因为虽则我巴不得如此，我却不敢担保能把他的行止源源本本地告知于你。你不在期间，我若接到他的电报，当然即刻转给你。我要你找到皮尔斯，但无论如何，不许让美国的警方知道你在追踪他，也不许他们疑心他的行动有什么见不得人。我想要弄清的不过是：他可曾结下什么不相宜的姻缘？他可还是自由之身？我汇给他的钱，可曾用在他自己身上？对了，他告诉我，他在世界各地买下了一批实业股份，其中有一部分股票就存在我手里。可另有一大批我对不上账，我去函查问，他回话说都稳稳当当地存放在南非的一家银行里了。我之所以要你把这件事整个儿烂在肚里，是因为——你自会明白——我不能让他被当地当局的殷勤照拂弄得下不来台。还有一层，我千叮万嘱的是，绝不能叫他知道是我派你去的。怎么样，马丁先生，这个差事合你的心意么？”

===Original===

Dick smiled.

“It looks to me like a very pleasant sort of holiday. How long will this chase last?”

===Chinese===

迪克笑了。

“在我看来，这倒像是一场颇为惬意的度假。这趟追寻要延续多久？”

===Original===

“I don’t know﻿—a few months, a few weeks: it all depends upon the report I receive from you, which, by the way, must be cabled to me direct. I have a very free hand and I can allow you the limit of expenses; in addition to which I will pay you a handsome fee.”

He named a sum which was surprisingly munificent.

===Chinese===

“说不准——几个月，几个星期，全看你给我的报告行事；顺便交代一句，报告必须直接拍电报给我。我手中权限极宽，经费任你花到上限为止；此外我还将付你一笔可观的酬金。”

他报出的那个数目，阔绰得出人意料。

===Original===

“When would you want me to go?”

===Chinese===

“您要我什么时候动身？”

===Original===

The lawyer took out a little pocketbook and evidently consulted a calendar.

“Today is Wednesday; suppose you leave next Wednesday by the Cunarder? At present he is in Boston, but he tells me that he is going to New York, where he will be staying at the Commodore. Boston is a favourite hunting ground of his.” His lips twitched. “I believe he intends sparing a chapter to the American War of Independence,” he said dryly; “and, naturally, Boston will afford him an excellent centre for that study.”

===Chinese===

律师掏出一本小记事簿，显然是查了查日历。

“今天是星期三；就下星期三搭冠达邮轮（Cunarder）动身，如何？他眼下在波士顿，不过他告诉我说要去纽约，届时将下榻科莫多旅馆（the Commodore）。波士顿是他中意的一处猎场。”他嘴唇微微一抽。“我相信，他打算为美国独立战争匀出一章的篇幅。”他干巴巴地说，“而波士顿呢，自然会给这项研究提供一个绝好的根据地。”

===Original===

“One question,” said Dick, as he rose to go. “Have you any reason to suppose that he has contracted, as you say, an undesirable alliance﻿—in other words, has married somebody that he shouldn’t have married?”

===Chinese===

“还有一问。”迪克起身告辞时说，“您可有半点根据，疑心他结下了您所说的不相宜的姻缘——换句话说，娶了不该娶的人？”

===Original===

“No reason at all, except my suspicious mind,” smiled Mr. Havelock. “If you become friendly with him, as I am perfectly sure with an effort you can succeed in doing, there are certain things I would like you to urge upon him. The first of these is that he come back to England and takes his seat in the House of Peers. That is very essential. Then I should like him to have a London season, because it’s high time he was married and off my mind. Selford Manor is going to ruin for want of an occupant. It is disgraceful that a fine old house like that should be left to the charge of a caretaker﻿—anyway, he ought to come back to be buried there,” he added, with a certain grim humour, and Dick did not quite understand the point of his remark until eight months later.

===Chinese===

“毫无根据，只怪我这颗多疑的心。”哈夫洛克先生含着笑说。“你若能同他结交成朋友——我深信只要你肯下功夫，这事准能办到——有几件事我要请你劝一劝他。头一件，便是要他回英国来，到上议院（House of Peers）里就席履职。这一节至关重要。其次，我盼着他回伦敦过一个社交季，因为他早该成家，也好叫我卸下这桩心事。塞尔福德庄园无人居住，正日渐倾颓。那样一座精美老宅，竟交给一个看管人照应，实在不成体统——不管怎么说，他总该回来，将来葬在那儿。”他补了这么一句，带着几分冷峻的诙谐，而迪克要过八个月，才全然明白这话的用意。

===Original===

The task was, in Dr. Stalletti’s words, extraordinary and bizarre, but it was not wholly unusual. Indeed, the first thought he had was its extreme simplicity. The commission was really a holiday on a grand scale, and something of his regret at leaving Scotland Yard was expunged by the pleasant prospect.

===Chinese===

用斯塔莱蒂医生（Dr. Stalletti）的话来说，这桩差事离奇古怪，却也并非全然罕见。说实在的，他心头泛起的第一个念头，倒是它简单到了极处。这趟委托分明是一场大规模的度假，一想到那美妙的前景，他告别苏格兰场的几分憾意，也就被冲刷干净了。

===Original===

It was nine o’clock on this wet October night when he came into Acacia Road. There was not a cab in sight, and he had to walk half a mile before he reached a rank. Letting himself into his flat, he found it in darkness, and to his surprise Pheeney had gone. The remains of the dinner were on the table﻿—he had told the housekeeper that he would clear the board, but one corner of the tablecloth had been turned up, and there on the cleared space half a dozen sheets of paper and a fountain pen. Evidently Lew intended returning, but though Dick Martin waited up until two o’clock, there was no sign of the grave-robber. For some reason Pheeney had changed his mind.

===Chinese===

这天十月之夜细雨绵绵，九点钟他才踏进阿卡西亚路。街上连一辆出租车的影子也没有，他步行了半英里才寻到一处候客站。他开门进了自己的公寓，屋里一片黢黑，出乎意料，菲尼已经走了。残席还摆在桌上——他先前同管家说过，桌面由他自理——但桌布一角被翻了上来，腾出的空处搁着六张信纸和一支自来水笔。看那光景，卢是打算回来的，然而迪克·马丁一直守到凌晨两点，也没见那个盗坟贼的半点踪影。不知怎的，菲尼变了卦。

===Original===

At half past ten the next morning he called at the library with his book. The girl looked up with a little laugh as he came in.

===Chinese===

第二天上午十点半，他揣着那本书来到图书馆。他一进门，姑娘便抬头浅浅一笑。

===Original===

“I admit I’m a good joke,” he said ruefully. “Here is your book. It was taken by an ignorant foreigner, who believed that loaning libraries are run on rather haphazard lines.”

She stared at the book. “Really, you are most impressive, Mr. Martin. Please tell me how you did it.”

===Chinese===

“我承认我自己就是个大笑话。”他懊恼地说，“书在这儿。拿走它的是个无知的外国人，只当租借图书馆办事是可以随心所欲、毫无章程的。”

她盯着那本书。“您可真叫人叹服，马丁先生。请告诉我，您是怎么办到的？”

===Original===

“Sheer deduction,” he said gaily. “I knew the man who took it was a foreigner, because you told me so. I guessed his address because you gave it me; and I recovered the book by the intricate process of asking for it!”

“Wonderful!” she breathed, and they laughed together.

===Chinese===

“全凭推理。”他眉飞色舞地说，“我知道拿书的是个外国人，因为是您告诉我的。我猜中了他的住址，因为是您给我的；至于把书追回来，靠的是一道繁复工序——张口讨要！”

“妙极了！”她轻声赞叹，两人一齐笑了起来。

===Original===

There was small excuse for his lingering, yet he contrived, as she hinted rather plainly, to hinder her for the greater part of an hour. Happily, the patrons of the Bellingham Library were not early risers, and she had the best part of the morning to herself.

===Chinese===

他赖着不走，本没有多少借口，可他偏有本事缠了她大半个钟头——她话里话外已点得相当明白了。所幸贝灵厄姆图书馆的主顾们都不是早起的人，上午这段大好时光，原是她独自消受的。

===Original===

“I am going abroad next week for a few months,” he said carelessly. “I don’t know why I tell you, but I thought possibly you would be interested in foreign travel.”

===Chinese===

“下星期我要出国几个月。”他淡淡地说，“我也说不清为什么告诉您，只是想着，您或许会对异国游历感兴趣。”

===Original===

She smiled to herself.

“You are certainly the naivest detective I have ever met! In fact, the only detective I have ever met!” she added. Then, seeing his obvious discomfiture, she became almost kind. “You see, Mr. Martin, I have been very well brought up”﻿—even in her kindness, her irony made him wince﻿—“which means that I am fearfully conventional. I wonder if you can guess how many men one meets in the course of a week who try to interest you in their family affairs? I’m not being unkind really,” she smiled, as he protested.

===Chinese===

她暗自莞尔。

“您无疑是我平生所见最天真的侦探！说实在的，也是我平生所见的唯一一位侦探！”她补了一句。随即瞧见他一脸的窘态，她差不多算得上和气了。“您瞧，马丁先生，我是家教顶好的人”——话虽和气，那份讥诮仍刺得他脸皮发紧——“这就是说，我守起旧礼数来怕人。您可猜得到，一个礼拜里能撞见多少个男人，变着法儿想教您对他们家里的事上心？我这可不是刻薄。”见他要分辩，她笑了笑。

===Original===

“I’ve been rather a brute﻿—I’m awfully sorry,” said Dick frankly, “and I deserve all the roasting you give me. But it’s very natural that even a humble detective officer should wish to improve an acquaintance with one who, if I may say so without bringing a blush to your maiden cheeks, has a singularly attractive mind.”

===Chinese===

“我方才实在是个粗坯——抱歉之至。”迪克坦率地说，“您怎么数落我，我都认了。不过，就连一个不起眼的小侦探，也想同一位小姐把交情往深里处一处——那位小姐头脑之动人，恕我拣着不叫您闺颜飞红的话说，实在世间少有——这不是再自然不过的事么？”

===Original===

“And now let us all be complimentary,” she said, though the colour in her face was heightened and her eyes were a little brighter. “You are the world’s best detective, and if ever I lose anything, I am sending immediately for you.”

===Chinese===

“得了，现在咱们彼此恭维一番吧。”她说，尽管脸上的红晕深了几分，眼波也亮了几分。“您是天下第一神探，往后我但凡丢了什么，立刻打发人去请您。”

===Original===

“Then you’ll draw a blank,” said Dick triumphantly. “I’m leaving the force and becoming a respectable member of society tomorrow, Miss﻿—?”

===Chinese===

“那您可要空手而回了。”迪克扬扬得意地说，“我明天就离职，从明天起就是社会上一位体面人物了，小姐——您贵姓？”

===Original===

She did not attempt to help him.

Then suddenly he saw a look of understanding come to her face.

===Chinese===

她并不打算帮他一把。

忽然，他看见她脸上浮起一层恍然的神色。

===Original===

“You’re not the man that Mr. Havelock is sending to look for my relative, are you?”

“Your relative?” he asked in amazement. “Is Lord Selford a relative of yours?”

===Chinese===

“您该不会就是哈夫洛克先生要派去寻我亲戚的那位吧？”

“您的亲戚？”他愕然问道，“塞尔福德勋爵是您的亲戚？”

===Original===

She nodded.

“He’s a forty-second cousin, heaven knows how many times removed. Father was his second cousin. Mother and I were dining with Mr. Havelock the other night, and he said that he was trying to get a man to run Selford to earth.”

===Chinese===

她点了点头。

“他是我的第四十二重表亲，天知道隔了多少代。父亲同他是二等表亲。前几天母亲和我正陪着哈夫洛克先生吃饭，他说正想物色一个人，把塞尔福德给刨出来呢。”

===Original===

“Have you ever met him?” asked Dick.

She shook her head.

===Chinese===

“您见过他么？”迪克问。

她摇了摇头。

===Original===

“No, but my mother knew him when he was a small boy. I think she saw him once. His father was a horror. I suppose Mr. Havelock has told you that﻿—I am assuming that my guess is right: you are going in search of him?”

===Chinese===

“没见过，不过我母亲在他还是个小娃娃的时候认识他，我记得她见过他一面。他父亲是个可怕的人物。这事哈夫洛克先生想必已经对您说过了——我这是假定自己猜对了：您就是要动身去找他的？”

===Original===

Dick nodded.

“That was the sad news I was trying to break to you,” he said.

===Chinese===

迪克点了点头。

“我方才想婉转奉告的，正是这桩伤心事。”他说。

===Original===

At that moment their tête-à-tête was interrupted by the arrival of an elderly gentleman with a vinegary voice, who, Dick guessed, was the secretary.

===Chinese===

正在这时，一位嗓音酸溜溜的老先生驾到，打断了他们这番对谈——迪克揣度，此人便是那位秘书。

===Original===

He went back to Scotland Yard to find Captain Sneed, who had been absent when he had called on the phone that morning. Sneed listened without comment to the extraordinary story of Lew Pheeney’s midnight occupation.

===Chinese===

他回到苏格兰场去找斯尼德上尉——早晨他打电话过去时，上尉恰好不在。斯尼德听罢卢·菲尼午夜行当的离奇故事，未加一句评点。

===Original===

“It certainly sounds like a lie, and anything that sounds like a lie generally is a lie,” he said. “Why didn’t Pheeney stay, if he’d got this thing on his conscience? And who was chasing him? Did you see anybody?”

===Chinese===

“这事听上去透着像谎话，而凡是听着像谎话的，十之八九便是谎话。”他说，“菲尼要是心里真搁着这桩事，他干吗不留下来？还有，是谁在追他？你可瞧见什么人了？”

===Original===

“Nobody,” said Dick. “But the man was afraid, and genuinely so.”

“Humph!” said Sneed, and pressed a bell.

===Chinese===

“没瞧见。”迪克说，“可那人当时是害怕，货真价实的害怕。”

“哼！”斯尼德应了一声，按了按铃。

===Original===

To the clerk who answered:

“Send a man to pick up Pheeney and bring him here. I want to ask him a few questions,” he said. And then, calling the man back: “You know his address, Dick. Go along and see if you can unearth him.”

===Chinese===

对着前来应铃的文书，他说：

“派个人去把菲尼接来，带到这儿，我有几个问题要问他。”接着又把那人唤了回来：“他的住址你知道，迪克。你自己跑一趟，看看能不能把他挖出来。”

===Original===

“My term of service expires at twelve today.”

“Midnight,” said Sneed laconically. “Get busy!”

===Chinese===

“我今天十二点任期便满了。”

“是半夜十二点。”斯尼德言简意赅地说，“这就干活去吧！”

===Original===

Lew Pheeney lived in Great Queen Street, at a lodging he had occupied for years; but his landlady could give no information. Pheeney had left the previous afternoon somewhere about five and had not returned. A haunt of the burglar was a small club, extensively patronized by the queer class which hovers eternally on the rim of the law. Pheeney had not been there﻿—he usually came in to breakfast and to collect his letters.

===Chinese===

卢·菲尼住在大女王街（Great Queen Street）一处他赁居多年的寓所，但房东太太提供不出半点消息：菲尼前一天下午五点来钟出的门，此后再没回来。这窃贼惯常出没的另一个去处，是一家小俱乐部，长年被那号永远在法律边缘上打转的古怪角色挤得满满当当。菲尼没有去过那儿——他往常总要到那里用早饭、取信件。

===Original===

Dick saw a man who said he had had an engagement with Pheeney on the previous night, and that he had waited until twelve.

“Where am I likely to find him?”

===Chinese===

迪克见到一个人，说是前一晚同菲尼有约在先，直等到十二点也没见人影。

“我上哪儿才寻得着他呢？”

===Original===

Here, however, no information was forthcoming. Dick Martin’s profession was as well known as Mr. Pheeney’s.

He reported the result of his visits to Sneed, who for some reason took a more serious view of the whole matter than Dick had expected.

===Chinese===

不过这人也拿不出什么消息。迪克·马丁干的这一行，同菲尼先生干的那一行一样，是无人不晓的。

他把走访结果报给斯尼德；不知怎的，斯尼德对整桩事情的看法，比迪克预想的要郑重得多。

===Original===

“I’m believing it now, that grave-robbing story,” said Sneed, “and certainly it’s remarkable if Lew was upset, because nothing short of an earthquake would raise a squeal with him. Maybe he’s at your flat?”

===Chinese===

“那桩盗坟的事，我如今信了几分。”斯尼德说，“卢若是当真慌了神，那可真是桩奇事——不闹地震，是听不见他哼半声的。会不会他上你公寓去了？”

===Original===

When Dick got home the flat was empty. His housekeeper had neither seen nor heard from the visitor. The detective strolled into his bedroom, pulled off his coat, intending to put on the old shooting jacket he wore when he was writing﻿—for he had a number of reports to finish before he made his final exit from the Yard.

===Chinese===

迪克回到家，公寓里空无一人。管家既没见过那位来客，也没接到过他的只字音讯。侦探踱进卧室，脱下身上外衣，想换上他写作时惯穿的那件旧猎装——因为在最后走出苏格兰场之前，他还有好几份报告要赶完。

===Original===

The coat was not hanging up where it was usually kept, and he remembered that his housekeeper had told him that she had put it in the bureau: a tall piece of mahogany furniture where his four suits were invariably hung on hangers.

===Chinese===

那件上衣没有挂在平日搁它的地方；他这才记起，管家同他说过，她把它收进衣橱里去了：那是一件高大的红木家具，他的四套西装向来都用衣架挂在里面。

===Original===

Without a thought he turned the handle of the bureau door and pulled it open. As he did so, the body of a man fell against him, almost knocking him over, and dropped to the floor with an inanimate thud. It was Lew Pheeney, and he was dead.

===Chinese===

他不假思索地拧动衣橱的把手，一拉而开。便在这一拉之间，一具人的躯体直撞进他怀里，险些把他撞了个跟头，随即一声没了活气的闷响，颓然倒在地板上。那是卢·菲尼。他已经死了。

## V / 五

===Original===

The big five at Scotland Yard filled Dick Martin’s dining-room, waiting for the verdict of the medical man who had been hastily summoned. The doctor came in in a few minutes.

===Chinese===

苏格兰场的五位巨头坐满了迪克·马丁家的饭厅，等着那位被匆匆召来的医生给出论断。几分钟后，医生便进来了。

===Original===

“So far as I can tell by a superficial examination,” he said, “he’s been dead for some hours, and was either strangled or his neck was broken.”

In spite of his self-control, Dick shivered. He had slept in the room that night, where, behind the polished door, lay that ghastly secret.

===Chinese===

“就我从表面检查所能判断的来看，”他说，“人已经死了好几个钟头，要么是被勒死的，要么是脖颈被折断了。”

迪克纵然自持力强，也不禁打了个寒噤。头天夜里，他就睡在那套房间里，而就在那里，一扇擦得锃亮的门背后，躺着那个骇人的秘密。

===Original===

“There was no sign of a struggle, Martin?” asked one of the officers.

“None whatever,” said Dick emphatically. “I am inclined to agree with the doctor: I should think that he was struck by something heavy and killed instantly. But how they got into the flat, God knows!”

===Chinese===

“没有搏斗过的痕迹吧，马丁？”其中一位警官问道。

“半点都没有。”迪克斩钉截铁地说，“我倒是倾向于同意医生的看法：我想他是被什么沉重的家伙击中，当场毙命的。可他们究竟是怎么进的这套公寓，只有天知道！”

===Original===

Inquiries of the girl who worked the night elevator were unsatisfactory, because she could remember nobody having come into the flat after Dick had gone out.

The six detectives made a minute examination of the premises.

===Chinese===

盘问那位开夜班电梯的姑娘也没有结果，因为她想不起迪克出门之后有谁进过这套公寓。

六名侦探对寓所各处进行了细致入微的勘查。

===Original===

“There’s only one way he could have come in,” said Sneed when the inspection was over, “and that is through the kitchenette.”

There was a door in the kitchen leading to a tiny balcony, by the side of which ran an outside service lift, used, as Dick explained, to convey tradesmen’s parcels from the courtyard below, and worked from the ground level by a small handle and winch.

===Chinese===

“他要想进来，只有一条道，”检查结束后斯尼德说，“就是穿过那间小厨房。”

厨房里有一扇门通向一个小小的阳台，阳台旁边装着一部设在墙外的杂用升降机。据迪克解释，这部升降机是用来把商家送货的包裹从楼下的院子吊上来的，由地面上的一个小摇柄和绞盘操纵。

===Original===

“You don’t remember if this kitchen door was bolted?” asked Sneed.

The troubled young man explained that he had not been in the kitchen after his return on the previous night. But his housekeeper, who was hovering tearfully in the background, volunteered the information that the door was open when she had come that morning.

===Chinese===

“你记不记得厨房这扇门当时上没上闩？”斯尼德问。

这位心神不宁的年轻人解释说，头天夜里回来后，他没进过厨房。倒是他的女管家——她正噙着泪在一旁守着——主动说，那天早上她来时，那扇门是敞开的。

===Original===

Dick looked down into the yard. The flat was sixty feet from the ground, and although it was possible that the intruder had climbed the ropes of the service lift, it seemed a feat beyond the power of most burglars.

“He gave you no indication as to who the man was he feared?” asked Sneed, when the rest of the Yard officers had gone back to headquarters.

===Chinese===

迪克朝楼下的院子望去。这套公寓离地面有六十英尺，闯入的人纵然有可能攀着杂用升降机的绳索爬上来，这份身手也似乎远非一般窃贼所能企及。

“他没向你透露过他怕的那个人是谁吗？”等其他苏格兰场的警官都回总部去了之后，斯尼德问道。

===Original===

“No,” Dick shook his head. “He told me nothing. He was scared, and I’m sure his story was perfectly true﻿—namely, that he was engaged to rob a grave, and that he had an idea that the man who made the engagement would have killed him if he had succeeded in his task.”

Dick went down to Lincoln’s Inn Fields that morning and had an interview with Mr. Havelock, who had already read the account in the evening newspapers, though Lew’s strange story was suppressed by the police even at the inquest.

===Chinese===

“没有。”迪克摇了摇头，“他什么也没告诉我。他是吓坏了，而我确信他讲的事句句属实——也就是说，有人雇他去盗一座坟，而他有种感觉：他若真把差事办成，雇他的人就会要了他的命。”

那天上午，迪克去了林肯律师学院广场，同哈夫洛克先生面谈。哈夫洛克已在晚报上读到了报道，虽然警方连在验尸审讯时都把卢那段离奇的说法压了下来。

===Original===

“Yes, I was afraid this might interfere with our plans, but I’m not particular to a week or two, and if you must remain behind for inquiries, I will still further extend the period. Though in a sense the matter is urgent, it is not immediately so.”

There was a conference of Yard officials, and it was agreed that Dick should be allowed to leave England immediately after the inquest, unless an arrest was made, on the understanding that he was to keep in touch with headquarters, so that, should the murderer be found, it would be possible for him to return to give evidence at the trial. This arrangement he conveyed to Mr. Havelock.

The inquest was held on the Friday, and, after Dick’s evidence, adjourned for an indefinite period. On the Saturday morning at twelve o’clock he left England, on the wildest chase that any man had ever undertaken. And behind him, did he but know it, stalked the shadow of death.

===Chinese===

“是啊，我原本还担心这事会妨碍我们的计划呢，不过差上一两个星期我不在意；要是你必须留下来配合查问，我可以把期限再放宽一些。这件事虽然在某种意义上很紧要，倒也不是迫在眉睫。”

苏格兰场的官员们开了一个会，商定允许迪克在验尸审讯之后立即离开英国——除非在此之前有人落网——条件是他必须同总部保持联络，这样一旦凶手被拿获，他仍能赶回来出庭作证。他把这一安排转告了哈夫洛克先生。

验尸审讯于星期五开庭，迪克作证之后，审讯便无限期休会。星期六上午十二点，他离开了英国，踏上了世人所曾从事过的最为疯狂的一场追逐。而他浑然不知，在他身后，正有一道死亡的阴影悄然潜随。

## VI / 六

===Original===
When Dick Martin left England on his curious quest, the Pheeney murder bulked largely in the newspapers, and almost as largely in his mind. There were other thoughts and other fancies to occupy the voyage, and, long after the memory of the murdered cracksman had faded, there remained with him the vision of two grey eyes that were laughing at him all the time, and the sound of a low, sweet, teasing voice.

If he had only had the sense to discover her name before he left. He might have written to her, or, or least, sent her picture postcards of the strange lands through which he travelled. But, in the hurry of his departure, and occupied as he was with the Pheeney crime, though he played no official part in the inevitable inquiries which followed, he had neither the time nor the excuse to call upon her. A letter addressed to “the pretty lady with the grey eyes at the Bellingham Library” might conceivably reach her, if there were no other lady employed in the building who also was favoured with eyes of that hue. On the other hand (he argued this quite gravely, as though it were an intelligent proposition) she might conceivably be annoyed.

From Chicago he sent a letter to the secretary of the library, enclosing a subscription, though he had no more need of scientific volumes than a menagerie of wild cats. But he hoped that he would see her name on the receipt﻿—it was not until the letter was posted that he realized that by the time the receipt returned to Chicago he would be thousands of miles away, and cursed himself for his folly.
===Chinese===
迪克·马丁怀着那桩古怪的差事离开英国时，菲尼凶杀案正占据着报纸的大幅版面，在他心里也占了差不多大的分量。航程中自有别的心思、别的遐想来占据他；那位惨遭毒手的窃贼，在他的记忆里早已淡去许久之后，始终萦绕在他心头的，是一双总在嘲笑他的灰眼睛的幻影，和一个低柔甜润、逗弄人的嗓音。

唉，他要是动身前有那份心眼儿，打听出她的名字就好了。那样他还可以给她写信，或者退一步，从旅途经过的那些陌生地方给她寄几张风景明信片。可是行色匆匆，加上满脑子都是菲尼的案子——虽说在随后那场免不了的侦讯里他并未担任什么正式角色——他既没有时间、也寻不着借口去拜访她。一封写明“贝灵厄姆图书馆灰眼睛的漂亮小姐收”的信，倒未必不能送到她手里——只要那栋楼里没有第二位女士也生着一双这种颜色的眼睛。可话说回来（他一本正经地推想着，仿佛这是个聪明主张），她也可能因此着恼。

他从芝加哥（Chicago）给图书馆的秘书寄去一封信，随信附上一笔订阅费，尽管他对科学书刊的需要，同一笼野猫也差不了多少。他指望的是能在收据上看到她的名字——直到信寄出去他才想起，等收据寄回芝加哥时，他早已远在几千英里之外了，气得直骂自己糊涂。

===Original===
Naturally he heard nothing from Sneed, and was compelled to depend upon such stray English papers as came his way to discover how the Pheeney mystery had developed. Apparently the police had made no arrest, and the record of the crime had dwindled to small paragraphs in odd corners of the newspapers.

He came to Cape Town from Buenos Aires, to miss his man by a matter of days, and there had the first cheerful news he had received since his search began. It was a cable from Havelock asking him to return home at once, and with a joyful heart he boarded the Castle boat at the quay. On that day he made his second important discovery, the first having been made in Buenos Aires.

In all his travels he had not once come up with the will-o’-the-wisp lordling whom he had followed half round the world, and the zest of the chase had already departed from him. From Cape Town to Madeira was a thirteen-day voyage by the intermittent steamer on which he travelled, having missed the mail by four days. To a man with other interests than deck sports, the peculiar characteristics of passengers, and the daily sweepstake, those thirteen days represented the dullest period Dick Martin had ever endured. And then, when the ship stopped to coal, the miracle happened. Just before the steamer left, a launch came alongside; half a dozen passengers mounted the stairs, and for a moment Dick thought he was dreaming.
===Chinese===
自然，斯尼德那边音讯全无，他只好靠手边偶尔撞见的几份英国报纸，来了解菲尼之谜有何进展。显然警方没有抓人，这桩罪案的报道已缩成了报纸边边角角上的短简小讯。

他从布宜诺斯艾利斯（Buenos Aires）来到开普敦（Cape Town），还是以几日之差，与追踪的目标失之交臂；在那里，他得到了自寻人启程以来的头一个喜讯：哈夫洛克拍来电报，要他即刻回国。他欢天喜地，在码头上登上了城堡航线的邮船。就在那一天，他有了第二个重大发现——第一个是在布宜诺斯艾利斯得的。

一路辗转，他始终没能追上那个行踪飘忽的贵族子弟——为了此人他已绕了大半个地球，追猎的兴头也早已消磨殆尽。从开普敦到马德拉（Madeira），乘他搭的这班不定期航行的邮船要走十三天，因为他误了邮船，晚了四天开船。对一个无意于甲板游戏、旅客群像和每日赛马抽彩的人来说，这十三天是迪克·马丁平生熬过的最乏味的一段日子。可就在船停靠加煤的时候，奇迹发生了。开船前一刻，一艘小艇靠上船舷；六七名乘客拾级而上，一时间迪克竟疑心自己在做梦。

===Original===
It was she! There was no mistaking her. He could have picked her out of a million. She did not see him, nor did he make himself known to her. For now that they were, so to speak, under one roof, and the opportunity that he dreamed of had presented itself in such an unexpected fashion, he was curiously shy, and avoided her until almost the last day of the voyage.

She was coolness itself when at last they met.

“Oh yes, I knew you were on board. I saw your name in the passenger list,” she said, and he was so agitated that he did not even resent the amusement in her eyes.
===Chinese===
